<section id="S3" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">3 </span>An <math id="S3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Omega(n^{3/2-\epsilon})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝛀</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo class="ltx_mathvariant_bold" mathvariant="bold" stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>𝒏</mi><mrow><mrow><mn>𝟑</mn><mo class="ltx_mathvariant_bold" mathvariant="bold">/</mo><mn>𝟐</mn></mrow><mo class="ltx_mathvariant_bold" mathvariant="bold">−</mo><mi class="ltx_mathvariant_bold-italic" mathvariant="bold-italic">ϵ</mi></mrow></msup><mo class="ltx_mathvariant_bold" mathvariant="bold" stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Omega(n^{3/2-\epsilon})</annotation></semantics></math> Conditional Lower Bound for Average Linkage HAC</h2>

<div id="S3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In this section, we show an <math id="S3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Omega(n^{3/2-\epsilon})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Omega(n^{3/2-\epsilon})</annotation></semantics></math> conditional lower bound on the time required to solve average linkage HAC on general weighted graphs. Specifically, we show this lower bound assuming the Combinatorial Boolean Matrix Multiplication (BMM) conjecture, a central conjecture in fine-grained complexity about the time required to multiply two <math id="S3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n\times n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n\times n</annotation></semantics></math> boolean matrices <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib43" title="" class="ltx_ref">43</a>, <a href="#bib.bib2" title="" class="ltx_ref">2</a>]</cite>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmconjecture1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_conjecture">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmconjecture1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Conjecture 1</span></span><span id="Thmconjecture1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"> </span>(Combinatorial BMM)<span id="Thmconjecture1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmconjecture1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmconjecture1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmconjecture1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Combinatorial algorithms cannot solve Boolean Matrix Multiplication in time <math id="Thmconjecture1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n^{3-\epsilon})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n^{3-\epsilon})</annotation></semantics></math> for <math id="Thmconjecture1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p2" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S3.p2.1" class="ltx_p">We refer to <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib2" title="" class="ltx_ref">2</a>]</cite> for an in-depth discussion of the somewhat informal notion of “combinatorial” algorithms and more on Conjecture <a href="#Thmconjecture1" title="Conjecture 1 (Combinatorial BMM). ‣ 3 An 
        
          
            Ω
            
              
              
                (
                
                  
                  n
                  
                    -
                    
                      /
                      3
                      2
                    
                    ϵ
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Conditional Lower Bound for Average Linkage HAC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> and its history.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p3.1" class="ltx_p">In this work we will make use of an equivalent characterization of the BMM conjecture due to <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib43" title="" class="ltx_ref">43</a>]</cite>. Specifically, <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib43" title="" class="ltx_ref">43</a>]</cite> shows that the BMM problem is sub-cubically equivalent to the Triangle Detection problem: the problem of deciding whether or not an input graph <math id="S3.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> contains a triangle (i.e., cycle of <math id="S3.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="3" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>3</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">3</annotation></semantics></math> vertices). The following summarizes this result.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmtheorem6" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_theorem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmtheorem6.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Theorem 6</span></span><span id="Thmtheorem6.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"> </span>(Theorem 1.3 of <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib43" title="" class="ltx_ref">43</a>]</cite>)<span id="Thmtheorem6.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmtheorem6.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmtheorem6.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmtheorem6.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Combinatorial algorithms cannot solve Triangle Detection in time <math id="Thmtheorem6.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n^{3-\epsilon})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n^{3-\epsilon})</annotation></semantics></math> for <math id="Thmtheorem6.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math> unless <a href="#Thmconjecture1" title="Conjecture 1 (Combinatorial BMM). ‣ 3 An 
        
          
            Ω
            
              
              
                (
                
                  
                  n
                  
                    -
                    
                      /
                      3
                      2
                    
                    ϵ
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Conditional Lower Bound for Average Linkage HAC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a> is false.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p4.1" class="ltx_p">Thus, we give a reduction from Triangle Detection to average linkage HAC. Our reduction will quadratically increase the number of vertices of the input Triangle Detection instance, and therefore give an <math id="S3.p4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Omega(n^{3/2-\epsilon})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Omega(n^{3/2-\epsilon})</annotation></semantics></math> lower bound for average linkage HAC. In the rest of this section, we show the following quadratic-blowup reduction from Triangle Detection to average linkage HAC.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmtheorem7" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_theorem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmtheorem7.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Theorem 7</span></span><span id="Thmtheorem7.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmtheorem7.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmtheorem7.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmtheorem7.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Given a Triangle Detection instance on graph <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> vertices and <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics></math> edges, there is a reduction that runs in <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(t^{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(t^{2})</annotation></semantics></math> time and constructs an instance of average linkage HAC on graph <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t+t^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t+t^{2}</annotation></semantics></math> vertices and <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{2}+m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{2}+m</annotation></semantics></math> edges.
Furthermore, given the sequence of merges performed by average linkage HAC on <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>, we can solve Triangle Detection on <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> in time <math id="Thmtheorem7.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(t^{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(t^{2})</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S3.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p5.1" class="ltx_p">As a corollary of this reduction and <a href="#Thmtheorem6" title="Theorem 6 (Theorem 1.3 of []). ‣ 3 An 
        
          
            Ω
            
              
              
                (
                
                  
                  n
                  
                    -
                    
                      /
                      3
                      2
                    
                    ϵ
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Conditional Lower Bound for Average Linkage HAC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Theorem</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>, we obtain the following conditional lower-bound on the running time of HAC.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p6" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p6.1" class="ltx_p">See <a href="#Thmtheorem1" title="Theorem 1. ‣ 1.1 Our Contributions ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p7.1" class="ltx_p">As a second corollary, we obtain a conditional lower-bound in terms of the optimal running time of matrix multiplication for two <math id="S3.p7.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n\times n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n\times n</annotation></semantics></math> binary matrices.
Matrix multiplication can be solved in time <math id="S3.p7.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n^{\omega})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mi>ω</mi></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n^{\omega})</annotation></semantics></math> where <math id="S3.p7.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2\leq\omega&lt;2.3716" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>ω</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mn>2.3716</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2\leq\omega&lt;2.3716</annotation></semantics></math> <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib44" title="" class="ltx_ref">44</a>]</cite>.
An extensive line of research on matrix multiplication over the past thirty years has only improved <math id="S3.p7.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\omega" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ω</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\omega</annotation></semantics></math> from <math id="S3.p7.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2.376" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>2.376</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2.376</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="S3.p7.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2.3716" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>2.3716</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2.3716</annotation></semantics></math>, with the current state-of-the-art being due to a very recent result of Williams et al. <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib44" title="" class="ltx_ref">44</a>]</cite> (for a subset of the historical advances in this area see, e.g., <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib13" title="" class="ltx_ref">13</a>, <a href="#bib.bib27" title="" class="ltx_ref">27</a>, <a href="#bib.bib3" title="" class="ltx_ref">3</a>, <a href="#bib.bib42" title="" class="ltx_ref">42</a>]</cite>).
The fastest known algorithm for triangle detection works by simply reducing the problem to matrix multiplication and therefore runs in <math id="S3.p7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n^{\omega})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mi>ω</mi></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n^{\omega})</annotation></semantics></math> time.
Surprisingly, despite triangle detection only returning a single bit (whether a triangle exists or not in <math id="S3.p7.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>), the problem can be used to give a sub-cubic reduction for boolean matrix multiplication (where the output is <math id="S3.p7.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n^{2}</annotation></semantics></math> bits).
In particular, an algorithm for triangle detection running in time <math id="S3.p7.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n^{3-\delta})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>−</mo><mi>δ</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n^{3-\delta})</annotation></semantics></math> for some <math id="S3.p7.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\delta&gt;0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\delta&gt;0</annotation></semantics></math> yields an algorithm for matrix multiplication in time <math id="S3.p7.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n^{3-\delta/3})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>−</mo><mrow><mi>δ</mi><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n^{3-\delta/3})</annotation></semantics></math> <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib43" title="" class="ltx_ref">43</a>]</cite>.
Using this fact, we can derive a conditional lower bound based on the value of <math id="S3.p7.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\omega" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>ω</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\omega</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p8" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p8.1" class="ltx_p">See <a href="#Thmtheorem2" title="Theorem 2. ‣ 1.1 Our Contributions ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a></p>
</div>
<div id="S3.p9" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.p9.1" class="ltx_p">An interesting open question is whether there are faster non-combinatorial algorithms that can leverage fast matrix multiplication or Strassen-like techniques and improve over the <math id="S3.p9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Omega(n^{3/2-\epsilon})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Omega(n^{3/2-\epsilon})</annotation></semantics></math> barrier for combinatorial algorithms for average linkage HAC.</p>
</div>
<section id="S3.SS1" class="ltx_subsection">
<h3 class="ltx_title ltx_title_subsection"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_subsection">3.1 </span>Reduction</h3>

<div id="S3.SS1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We now prove Theorem <a href="#Thmtheorem7" title="Theorem 7. ‣ 3 An 
        
          
            Ω
            
              
              
                (
                
                  
                  n
                  
                    -
                    
                      /
                      3
                      2
                    
                    ϵ
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Conditional Lower Bound for Average Linkage HAC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">7</span></a> by
giving a quadratic-time reduction from triangle detection to average linkage HAC.
The reduction is loosely inspired by a recent lower-bound result for multidimensional range queries <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib26" title="" class="ltx_ref">26</a>]</cite>.
The input to the reduction is an unweighted graph <math id="S3.SS1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> on <math id="S3.SS1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> vertices with <math id="S3.SS1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics></math> edges; the problem is to detect whether <math id="S3.SS1.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> has a triangle.
To do this, we will construct a HAC instance on an edge-weighted graph <math id="S3.SS1.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S3.SS1.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t+t^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t+t^{2}</annotation></semantics></math> vertices and <math id="S3.SS1.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{2}+m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{2}+m</annotation></semantics></math> edges.
We will show that the specific way in which an exact HAC algorithm merges the edges in this instance reveals whether or not <math id="S3.SS1.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> has a triangle.</p>
</div>
<section id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Constructing <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math></h4>

<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Let <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N_{G}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{G}(v)</annotation></semantics></math> denote the neighbors of a vertex <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v\in G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>G</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v\in G</annotation></semantics></math> (note that <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v\notin N_{G}(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>∉</mo><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v\notin N_{G}(v)</annotation></semantics></math>).
We define <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> as follows.
We start by adding all vertices and edges from <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, that is the <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> vertices <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{1},\ldots,v_{t}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>t</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{1},\ldots,v_{t}</annotation></semantics></math> from <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, including all of their incident edges <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N_{G}(v_{i})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{G}(v_{i})</annotation></semantics></math>.
We call these the <span id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">core</span> vertices.
The initail weight of the edges between any two core vertices is set to <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.1" class="ltx_p">In addition to the core vertices, we add an additional <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{2}</annotation></semantics></math> <span id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">leaf</span> vertices that we connect to the core vertices with specific edge weights.
We add the <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{2}</annotation></semantics></math> leaf vertices over a sequence of <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>t</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t</annotation></semantics></math> <span id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">rounds</span> where the <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>-th round connects one new leaf vertex to every core vertex.
The weights to the newly added leaves depend on the neighbors of the node <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> in the original graph <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px1.p2.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math>, and are set as follows:</p>
<ol id="S3.I1" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="S3.I1.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">(<span id="S3.I1.i1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">1</span>)</span> 
<div id="S3.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.I1.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">A core vertex <math id="S3.I1.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> is connected to its new leaf with an edge of weight <math id="S3.I1.i1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1/i)-\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1/i)-\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> if <math id="S3.I1.i1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{j}\in N_{G}(v_{i})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{j}\in N_{G}(v_{i})</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S3.I1.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">(<span id="S3.I1.i2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">2</span>)</span> 
<div id="S3.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.I1.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">A core vertex <math id="S3.I1.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> is connected to its new leaf with an edge of weight <math id="S3.I1.i2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(1/i)+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1/i)+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math> if <math id="S3.I1.i2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{j}\notin N_{G}(v_{i})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>∉</mo><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{j}\notin N_{G}(v_{i})</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div></li>
</ol>
</div>

<section id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Running HAC on <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math></h4>

<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Having defined <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>, let us consider the merges that the exact HAC algorithm will make on this instance.
In this section, for succinctness we use <span id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">weight</span> to refer to the normalized (i.e., average linkage) weight.
HAC will begin by merging the maximum weight edges.
The maximum weight initially depends on the structure of <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N_{G}(v_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{G}(v_{1})</annotation></semantics></math>.
First, all core vertices that are not in <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N_{G}(v_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{G}(v_{1})</annotation></semantics></math> will merge with their round 1 leaves (since these edges have weight <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1+\epsilon" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1+\epsilon</annotation></semantics></math>) increasing their cluster size to <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>2</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2</annotation></semantics></math>.
This leaves all core vertices in <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N_{G}(v_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{G}(v_{1})</annotation></semantics></math>. Any edge in <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> between two such core vertices will have weight <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>, and will be merged next.
Crucially, any merge in this round with a weight of <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math> indicates a triangle incident on <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> since the two core vertex endpoints of the edge must be contained in <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N_{G}(v_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{G}(v_{1})</annotation></semantics></math>, hence connected by
edges to <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{1}</annotation></semantics></math>.
If no edges of weight <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math> merge, the remaining leaves that we added to core vertices in <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p1.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N_{G}(v_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>N</mi><mi>G</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N_{G}(v_{1})</annotation></semantics></math> merge into their neighboring core vertex.</p>
</div>
<div id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">Assuming we did not merge any weight <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math> edges in the first round, at this point the cluster size of each core vertex is <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>2</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2</annotation></semantics></math>, and so the weight of any edge originally in <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> (between two core vertices) will be <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1/4" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1/4</annotation></semantics></math>.
The edge weights to leaves in round <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>2</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2</annotation></semantics></math> will be <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="((1/2)\pm\epsilon)/2=1/4\pm(\epsilon/2)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>±</mo><mi>ϵ</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><mo>±</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">((1/2)\pm\epsilon)/2=1/4\pm(\epsilon/2)</annotation></semantics></math>, which is larger than <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1/4" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1/4</annotation></semantics></math> for edges of Type <a href="#S3.I1.i2" title="Item (2) ‣ Constructing 
            
              
                
                G
                ′ ‣ 3.1 Reduction ‣ 3 An 
        
          
            Ω
            
              
              
                (
                
                  
                  n
                  
                    -
                    
                      /
                      3
                      2
                    
                    ϵ
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Conditional Lower Bound for Average Linkage HAC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">(2)</span></a>.
Therefore, the same argument for how edges merge in round <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math> can be inductively applied to the next round.
The edge weights in round <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> for edges between core vertices will be <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1/i^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1/i^{2}</annotation></semantics></math>, and by the same argument as before, the Type <a href="#S3.I1.i2" title="Item (2) ‣ Constructing

       Conditional Lower Bound for Average Linkage HAC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">(1)</span></a>) edges will be larger (smaller) by <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\epsilon/i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>ϵ</mi><mo>/</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\epsilon/i</annotation></semantics></math>.
As a result of how an exact average linkage HAC will merge the edges of <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px2.p2.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>, we obtain the following lemma:</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmlemma1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmlemma1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma 1</span></span><span id="Thmlemma1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmlemma1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmlemma1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmlemma1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Consider the sequence of merges performed by the HAC algorithm on <math id="Thmlemma1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>. If the merge sequence consists of <math id="Thmlemma1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{2}</annotation></semantics></math> merges, which first merge all leaf vertices, and only then makes merges between core vertices, then <math id="Thmlemma1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> does not contain any triangles.
If the merge sequence merges any edge between two core vertices in the first <math id="Thmlemma1.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>t</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t^{2}</annotation></semantics></math> merges, then <math id="Thmlemma1.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> contains a triangle.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
</section>

Given a <span id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Triangle Detection</span> instance on <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p2.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> vertices we create a graph <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p2.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p2.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n^{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n^{2})</annotation></semantics></math> vertices and <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p2.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n^{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n^{2})</annotation></semantics></math> edges, and run HAC on <math id="S3.SS1.SSS0.Px3.p2.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>G</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>.

<section id="S4" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">4 </span>Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees</h2>

<div id="S4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In this section we prove that average linkage HAC is likely hard to parallelize by showing it is <span id="S4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hard even on low depth trees.
We begin with some preliminaries. The formal definition of <span id="S4.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hardness we will use is as follows.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmdefinition1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_definition">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmdefinition1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Definition 1</span></span><span id="Thmdefinition1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"> </span>(<span id="Thmdefinition1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-Hard)<span id="Thmdefinition1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmdefinition1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmdefinition1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmdefinition1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">A problem is <span id="Thmdefinition1.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hard if all problems of <span id="Thmdefinition1.p1.1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span> are logspace-reducible to it.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p2.1" class="ltx_p">For our purposes we will not need to define the class <span id="S4.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>. Rather, we only need the above definition of <span id="S4.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hardness and a single <span id="S4.p2.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hard problem, <span id="S4.p2.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span>. Recall that a matching of a graph <math id="S4.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V,E)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V,E)</annotation></semantics></math> is a subset of edges <math id="S4.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M\subseteq E" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>M</mi><mo>⊆</mo><mi>E</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M\subseteq E</annotation></semantics></math> if each vertex is incident to at most one edge of <math id="S4.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math>. A matching is said to be maximal if each <math id="S4.p2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e=\{u,v\}\not\in M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mo>∉</mo><mi>M</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e=\{u,v\}\not\in M</annotation></semantics></math> satisfies the property that either <math id="S4.p2.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math> or <math id="S4.p2.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> is incident to an edge of <math id="S4.p2.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math>. The greedy algorithm for maximal matching initializes <math id="S4.p2.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math> as <math id="S4.p2.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\emptyset" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi mathvariant="normal">∅</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\emptyset</annotation></semantics></math> and then simply iterates over the edges of <math id="S4.p2.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="E" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>E</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">E</annotation></semantics></math> in some order and adds the current edge <math id="S4.p2.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>e</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="S4.p2.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="M" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>M</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">M</annotation></semantics></math> if the result of doing so is a matching.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmproblem1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_problem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmproblem1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Problem 1</span></span><span id="Thmproblem1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"> </span>(<span id="Thmproblem1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span>)<span id="Thmproblem1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmproblem1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmproblem1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmproblem1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">An instance of lexicographically first maximal matching (LFM Matching) consists of a bipartite graph <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V=L\sqcup R,E)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>⊔</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V=L\sqcup R,E)</annotation></semantics></math> with vertices ordered as <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="L=(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{n-1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>l</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>l</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L=(l_{0},l_{1},\ldots,l_{n-1})</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R=(r_{0},r_{1},\ldots,r_{n-1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R=(r_{0},r_{1},\ldots,r_{n-1})</annotation></semantics></math>. The lexicographically first maximal matching is the matching obtained by running the greedy algorithm for maximal matching on edges ordered first by their endpoint in <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>L</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L</annotation></semantics></math> and then by their endpoint in <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>R</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R</annotation></semantics></math>. That is, in this ordering <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e=\{l_{i},r_{j}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><msub><mi>l</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e=\{l_{i},r_{j}\}</annotation></semantics></math> precedes <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e^{\prime}=\{l_{i^{\prime}},r_{j^{\prime}}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>e</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi>i</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><msup><mi>j</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></msub><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e^{\prime}=\{l_{i^{\prime}},r_{j^{\prime}}\}</annotation></semantics></math> iff (1) <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i&lt;i^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi>i</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i&lt;i^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> or (2) <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i=i^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><msup><mi>i</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i=i^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="Thmproblem1.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j&lt;j^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><msup><mi>j</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j&lt;j^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>. Our goal is to decide if a designated input edge is in the LFM Matching.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p3.1" class="ltx_p">The following summarizes known hardness of <span id="S4.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmtheorem8" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_theorem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmtheorem8.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Theorem 8</span></span><span id="Thmtheorem8.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"> </span>(<cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib39" title="" class="ltx_ref">39</a>, <a href="#bib.bib29" title="" class="ltx_ref">29</a>]</cite>)<span id="Thmtheorem8.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmtheorem8.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmtheorem8.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmtheorem8.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif ltx_font_italic">LFM Matching<span id="Thmtheorem8.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_serif"> is <span id="Thmtheorem8.p1.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hard.</span></span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p4.1" class="ltx_p">Next, we introduce the search variant of the HAC problem whose <span id="S4.p4.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hardness we will prove.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmproblem2" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_problem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmproblem2.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Problem 2</span></span><span id="Thmproblem2.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"> </span>(<span id="Thmproblem2.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Average Linkage HAC</span>)<span id="Thmproblem2.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmproblem2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmproblem2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmproblem2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">An instance of <span id="Thmproblem2.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Average Linkage HAC</span> consists an undirected graph <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V,E)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>V</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V,E)</annotation></semantics></math>, along with edge weights <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w:E\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo lspace="0.278em" rspace="0.278em">:</mo><mrow><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">→</mo><msub><mi>ℝ</mi><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≥</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w:E\to\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}</annotation></semantics></math>. Consider the sequence <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(C_{1},C_{1}^{\prime}),(C_{2},C_{2}^{\prime}),\dots" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(C_{1},C_{1}^{\prime}),(C_{2},C_{2}^{\prime}),\dots</annotation></semantics></math> of cluster merges produced by the procedure </span><span id="Thmproblem2.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_typewriter">HAC(<em id="Thmproblem2.p1.1.2.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_serif ltx_font_italic"><math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math></em>)</span><span id="Thmproblem2.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic"> from Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 2 Preliminaries ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>. Given any pair of vertices <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u,v\in G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>G</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u,v\in G</annotation></semantics></math>, the goal of the <span id="Thmproblem2.p1.1.3.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Average Linkage HAC</span> problem is to output the index <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> such that <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u,v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u,v</annotation></semantics></math> first merge together at step <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>, namely <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u\in C_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u\in C_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v\in C_{i}^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>∈</mo><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>′</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v\in C_{i}^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> (or <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u\in C_{i}^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>∈</mo><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>′</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u\in C_{i}^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="Thmproblem2.p1.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v\in C_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>v</mi><mo>∈</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v\in C_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>).</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p5.1" class="ltx_p">To prove the hardness of <span id="S4.p5.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Average Linkage HAC</span>, we will first prove the hardness of an intermediate problem, called <span id="S4.p5.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span>. The construction of the <span id="S4.p5.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> problem will be more amenable to our reductions, and therefore simplify the following exposition. See <a href="#S4.F4" title="In 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> for an illustration of <span id="S4.p5.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmproblem3" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_problem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmproblem3.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Problem 3</span></span><span id="Thmproblem3.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold"> </span>(<span id="Thmproblem3.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span>)<span id="Thmproblem3.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmproblem3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmproblem3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmproblem3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">An instance of <span id="Thmproblem3.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> consists of a (<math id="Thmproblem3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0</annotation></semantics></math>-based indexed) <math id="Thmproblem3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n\times n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n\times n</annotation></semantics></math> matrix <math id="Thmproblem3.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> where each row contains a permutation of <math id="Thmproblem3.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{0,\ldots,n-1\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{0,\ldots,n-1\}</annotation></semantics></math> and some index <math id="Thmproblem3.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x\in[0,n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x\in[0,n)</annotation></semantics></math>.
The goal is to simulate the following algorithm.
Start with <math id="Thmproblem3.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I=\{0,\ldots,n-1\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I=\{0,\ldots,n-1\}</annotation></semantics></math> and execute the following steps for <math id="Thmproblem3.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i=0,\ldots,x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i=0,\ldots,x</annotation></semantics></math>:</span></p>
<ol id="S4.I1" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="S4.I1.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span> 
<div id="S4.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I1.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.I1.i1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Let </span><math id="S4.I1.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{i}=\argmin_{j\in I}A[i,j]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>arg</mi><mo lspace="0.170em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>min</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>I</mi></mrow></msub><mo>⁡</mo><mi>A</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{i}=\argmin_{j\in I}A[i,j]</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I1.i1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">.</span></p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I1.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span> 
<div id="S4.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I1.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.I1.i2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Set </span><math id="S4.I1.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I:=I\setminus\{k_{i}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>:=</mo><mrow><mi>I</mi><mo>∖</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I:=I\setminus\{k_{i}\}</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I1.i2.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">.</span></p>
</div></li>
</ol>
<p id="Thmproblem3.p1.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmproblem3.p1.2.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Our goal is to compute <math id="Thmproblem3.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{x}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{x}</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>

</figure></div><div class="ltx_flex_cell ltx_flex_size_many">
