<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S4.F4.sf5.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">(e)</span> </span><span id="S4.F4.sf5.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Step <math id="S4.F4.sf5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="4" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>4</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></figcaption>
</figure></div><div class="ltx_flex_cell ltx_flex_size_many">
<figure id="S4.F4.sf6" class="ltx_figure ltx_figure_panel ltx_align_center"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="/html/2404.14730/assets/aMin6.svg" id="S4.F4.sf6.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_square" width="476" height="547"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S4.F4.sf6.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">(f)</span> </span><span id="S4.F4.sf6.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Step <math id="S4.F4.sf6.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="5" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>5</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">5</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></figcaption>
</figure></div></div>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S4.F4.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 4</span>: </span><span id="S4.F4.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif" style="font-size:90%;">Adaptive Minimum<span id="S4.F4.5.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_serif"> on matrix <math id="S4.F4.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math>. The row considered in each step is shown in blue.
<math id="S4.F4.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> for the <math id="S4.F4.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>-th row written to the right of <math id="S4.F4.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> in green with witnessing entry of <math id="S4.F4.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> also in green. Indices removed from <math id="S4.F4.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>I</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I</annotation></semantics></math> in relevant rows crossed out in red.</span></span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S4.p6" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p6.1" class="ltx_p">Observe that both problems <a href="#Thmproblem2" title="Problem 2 (Average Linkage HAC). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a> and <a href="#Thmproblem3" title="Problem 3 (Adaptive Minimum). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> are defined as having an algorithm output an index <math id="S4.p6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i\in\{1,2,\dots\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>2</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i\in\{1,2,\dots\}</annotation></semantics></math>. Thus, these can be considered search problems instead of decision problems. We choose to work with the search versions of these problems for simplicity of our reductions, however, our reduction naturally extends to the decision variants (e.g., where the algorithm is given <math id="S4.p6.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u,v\in V" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u,v\in V</annotation></semantics></math> and an index <math id="S4.p6.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> and asked if <math id="S4.p6.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u,v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u,v</annotation></semantics></math> merge on step <math id="S4.p6.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>).</p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p7.1" class="ltx_p">We first prove the <span id="S4.p7.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hardness of this intermediate problem. See <a href="#S4.F5" title="In 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a> for an illustration of our reduction from <span id="S4.p7.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span> to <span id="S4.p7.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmlemma2" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmlemma2.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma 2</span></span><span id="Thmlemma2.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmlemma2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmlemma2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmlemma2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif ltx_font_italic">Adaptive Minimum<span id="Thmlemma2.p1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_serif"> is <span id="Thmlemma2.p1.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hard.</span></span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.2" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S4.p8" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p8.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p8.1.1" class="ltx_text">By <a href="#Thmtheorem8" title="Theorem 8 ([]). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Theorem</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">8</span></a> and the definition of <span id="S4.p8.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hardness (<a href="#Thmdefinition1" title="Definition 1 (CC-Hard). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Definition</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>), it suffices to argue that <span id="S4.p8.1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span> (<a href="#Thmproblem1" title="Problem 1 (LFM Matching). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>) is logspace reducible to <span id="S4.p8.1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> (<a href="#Thmproblem3" title="Problem 3 (Adaptive Minimum). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>).
We begin by describing our reduction and then observe that it only requires logarithmic space. The basic idea of the reduction is to associate with each vertex on the left side of our instance of <span id="S4.p8.1.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span> a row of the matrix of <span id="S4.p8.1.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> and each vertex on the right side of <span id="S4.p8.1.1.6" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span> a column of the matrix of <span id="S4.p8.1.1.7" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p9" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p9.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p9.1.1" class="ltx_text">More formally, consider an instance of <span id="S4.p9.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span> on graph <math id="S4.p9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G=(V=L\sqcup R,E)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>V</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>L</mi><mo>⊔</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo><mi>E</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G=(V=L\sqcup R,E)</annotation></semantics></math> where our goal is to decide if a given edge <math id="S4.p9.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>e</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e</annotation></semantics></math> is in the LFM matching.
We consider the following instance of <span id="S4.p9.1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> to solve this on a <math id="S4.p9.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2n\times 2n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">×</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2n\times 2n</annotation></semantics></math> size matrix <math id="S4.p9.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math>. We will refer to an index <math id="S4.p9.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> as a <em id="S4.p9.1.1.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">dummy index</em> if <math id="S4.p9.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i\geq n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>≥</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i\geq n</annotation></semantics></math>. We will construct the <math id="S4.p9.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>th row of <math id="S4.p9.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> to correspond to a permutation that first gives the indices of all neighbors of <math id="S4.p9.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="l_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>l</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">l_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="S4.p9.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>R</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R</annotation></semantics></math> sorted according to the ordering of <math id="S4.p9.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>R</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R</annotation></semantics></math> then gives all dummy indices then gives the indices of non-neighbors of <math id="S4.p9.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="l_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>l</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">l_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="S4.p9.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>R</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p10" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p10.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p10.1.1" class="ltx_text">More formally, for a vertex <math id="S4.p10.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="l_{i}\in L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>l</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><mi>L</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">l_{i}\in L</annotation></semantics></math>, we let <math id="S4.p10.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R_{i}\subseteq R" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>⊆</mo><mi>R</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R_{i}\subseteq R</annotation></semantics></math> be the vertices of <math id="S4.p10.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="G" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>G</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math> connected to <math id="S4.p10.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="l_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>l</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">l_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> and we let <math id="S4.p10.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{R_{i}}=R\setminus R_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>R</mi><mo>∖</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{R_{i}}=R\setminus R_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> be all vertices not adjacent to <math id="S4.p10.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="l_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>l</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">l_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>. Furthermore, for <math id="S4.p10.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{j}\in R_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{j}\in R_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>, we let <math id="S4.p10.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\text{ind}(r_{j})\in[|R_{i}|]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mtext>ind</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\text{ind}(r_{j})\in[|R_{i}|]</annotation></semantics></math> be the index of <math id="S4.p10.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="S4.p10.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> when we sort the vertices of <math id="S4.p10.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> according to the ordering on <math id="S4.p10.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>R</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R</annotation></semantics></math>. Note that, in general, it need not be the case that <math id="S4.p10.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\text{ind}(r_{j})=j" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mtext>ind</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\text{ind}(r_{j})=j</annotation></semantics></math>. Symmetrically, for a vertex <math id="S4.p10.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{j}\in\bar{R_{i}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><mover accent="true"><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>¯</mo></mover></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{j}\in\bar{R_{i}}</annotation></semantics></math>, we let <math id="S4.p10.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\text{ind}(r_{j})\in[|\bar{R_{i}}|]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mtext>ind</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><mover accent="true"><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>¯</mo></mover><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\text{ind}(r_{j})\in[|\bar{R_{i}}|]</annotation></semantics></math> be the index of <math id="S4.p10.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> when we sort <math id="S4.p10.m17" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\bar{R_{i}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mover accent="true"><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>¯</mo></mover><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\bar{R_{i}}</annotation></semantics></math> by the ordering on <math id="S4.p10.m18" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>R</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R</annotation></semantics></math>. Then, we define <math id="S4.p10.m19" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> as follows.</span></p>
<table id="S4.EGx1" class="ltx_equationgroup ltx_eqn_align ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody id="S4.Ex1"><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_eqn_cell"><math id="S4.Ex1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\displaystyle A[i,j]:=\begin{cases}\text{ind}(r_{j})&amp;\text{if $j&lt;n$ and $r_{j}\in R_{i}$}\\
j-n+|R_{i}|&amp;\text{if $j\geq n$}\\
\text{ind}(r_{j})+n+|R_{i}|&amp;\text{if $j&lt;n$ and $r_{j}\not\in R_{i}$}\end{cases}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>:=</mo><mrow><mo>{</mo><mtable columnspacing="5pt" rowspacing="0pt"><mtr><mtd class="ltx_align_left" columnalign="left"><mrow><mtext>ind</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mtd><mtd class="ltx_align_left" columnalign="left"><mrow><mtext>if </mtext><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mtext> and </mtext><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd class="ltx_align_left" columnalign="left"><mrow><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mtd><mtd class="ltx_align_left" columnalign="left"><mrow><mtext>if </mtext><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>≥</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow></mtd></mtr><mtr><mtd class="ltx_align_left" columnalign="left"><mrow><mrow><mtext>ind</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mrow></mtd><mtd class="ltx_align_left" columnalign="left"><mrow><mtext>if </mtext><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mtext> and </mtext><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo>∉</mo><msub><mi>R</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mrow></mtd></mtr></mtable></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle A[i,j]:=\begin{cases}\text{ind}(r_{j})&amp;\text{if $j&lt;n$ and $r_{j}\in R_{i}$}\\

\text{ind}(r_{j})+n+|R_{i}|&amp;\text{if $j&lt;n$ and $r_{j}\not\in R_{i}$}\end{cases}</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>

</div>
<div id="S4.p11" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p11.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p11.1.1" class="ltx_text">Lastly, let <math id="S4.p11.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math> (the index for which we would like to compute <math id="S4.p11.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{x}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{x}</annotation></semantics></math> in our instance of <span id="S4.p11.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span>) be the index of the endpoint of <math id="S4.p11.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>e</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="S4.p11.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="L" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>L</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">L</annotation></semantics></math>.
Once <span id="S4.p11.1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> computes <math id="S4.p11.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{x}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{x}</annotation></semantics></math>, we verify whether or not it corresponds to the endpoint of <math id="S4.p11.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>e</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e</annotation></semantics></math> in <math id="S4.p11.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="R" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>R</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">R</annotation></semantics></math> to determine the final output of the <span id="S4.p11.1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span> instance. Again, see <a href="#S4.F5" title="In 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p12" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p12.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p12.1.1" class="ltx_text">We now argue correctness of the reduction. A straightforward proof by induction on <math id="S4.p12.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> demonstrates that at the beginning of the <math id="S4.p12.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>th round of the <span id="S4.p12.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> algorithm we have that <math id="S4.p12.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>I</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I</annotation></semantics></math> consists of at least <math id="S4.p12.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i</annotation></semantics></math> dummy indices and <math id="S4.p12.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j&lt;n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j&lt;n</annotation></semantics></math> is not in <math id="S4.p12.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="I" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>I</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">I</annotation></semantics></math> only if <math id="S4.p12.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> is in the <span id="S4.p12.1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span> and is matched to some <math id="S4.p12.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="l_{i^{\prime}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>l</mi><msup><mi>i</mi><mo>′</mo></msup></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">l_{i^{\prime}}</annotation></semantics></math> for <math id="S4.p12.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i^{\prime}&lt;i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>i</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i^{\prime}&lt;i</annotation></semantics></math>. It follows that <math id="S4.p12.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e=(l_{i},r_{j})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>l</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e=(l_{i},r_{j})</annotation></semantics></math> is in the <span id="S4.p12.1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">LFM Matching</span> iff <math id="S4.p12.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{i}=j" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{i}=j</annotation></semantics></math>, showing correctness of our reduction.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p13" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p13.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p13.1.1" class="ltx_text">It remains to show that the above reduction can be done with logspace. In order to do so, we must argue that <math id="S4.p13.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A[i,j]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A[i,j]</annotation></semantics></math> can be computed with logspace for every <math id="S4.p13.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p13.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>j</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j</annotation></semantics></math>. Doing so is trivial given the above definition of <math id="S4.p13.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A[i,j]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A[i,j]</annotation></semantics></math>.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>

<div id="S4.p14" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p14.1" class="ltx_p">Concluding, we use the <span id="S4.p14.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hardness of <span id="S4.p14.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> to prove the <span id="S4.p14.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">CC</span>-hardness of <span id="S4.p14.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Average Linkage HAC</span>.
See <a href="#Thmtheorem3" title="Theorem 3. ‣ 1.1 Our Contributions ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.3" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S4.p15" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p15.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p15.1.1" class="ltx_text">Our reduction shows how to reduce an instance of <span id="S4.p15.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> (<a href="#Thmproblem3" title="Problem 3 (Adaptive Minimum). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>) of size <math id="S4.p15.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> to an instance of average linkage HAC on a tree.
We build a rooted tree, in which each root-to-leaf path has length <math id="S4.p15.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>2</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2</annotation></semantics></math> (i.e., the tree has depth <math id="S4.p15.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>2</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2</annotation></semantics></math>). We call the neighbors of the root <em id="S4.p15.1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">internal</em> nodes.
Observe that each node is either the root, an internal node or a leaf.
The fact that the tree is rooted is only for the convenience of the description.
In the construction, we will begin by assigning each node an initial size (see the definition of </span><span id="S4.p15.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">size</span><span id="S4.p15.1.3" class="ltx_text"> in Section <a href="#S2" title="2 Preliminaries ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>) which is possibly larger than <math id="S4.p15.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math> (but at most <math id="S4.p15.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{poly}(n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗈𝗅𝗒</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{poly}(n)</annotation></semantics></math>). We will later show how to remove these variable sizes, and reduce to the case where all nodes have initial size <math id="S4.p15.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math> (as in the original definition of HAC).</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p16" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p16.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p16.1.1" class="ltx_text">The basic idea of our construction is as follows. Our HAC instance will consist of a rooted tree where each child of the root corresponds to a column of <math id="S4.p16.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> in our <span id="S4.p16.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> instance. HAC merges will then happen in phases where each phase corresponds to a row of <math id="S4.p16.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math>. In a given phase, exactly one internal node will merge with the root which will correspond to this internal node’s column being minimum for the corresponding row of <math id="S4.p16.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math>. In order to guarantee this, each child of the root will have its own carefully selected children such that merging with these children guarantees the desired behavior in every phase.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p17" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p17.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p17.1.1" class="ltx_text">More formally, the tree is constructed as follows.
The root <math id="S4.p17.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math> of the tree has initial size <math id="S4.p17.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n^{8}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n^{8}</annotation></semantics></math>.
It has <math id="S4.p17.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> children, each of initial size <math id="S4.p17.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n^{4}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n^{4}</annotation></semantics></math>—we denote them by <math id="S4.p17.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{0},\ldots,v_{n-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{0},\ldots,v_{n-1}</annotation></semantics></math>.
The root is connected to its children using edges of weight <math id="S4.p17.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>, i.e., <math id="S4.p17.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w(r,v_{i})=1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>r</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w(r,v_{i})=1</annotation></semantics></math> for all <math id="S4.p17.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i=0,1,\dots,n-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i=0,1,\dots,n-1</annotation></semantics></math> (thus, the normalized weight of the edges <math id="S4.p17.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{rv_{i}\}_{i=0}^{n-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msubsup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{rv_{i}\}_{i=0}^{n-1}</annotation></semantics></math> are each <math id="S4.p17.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{n^{12}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>12</mn></msup></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{n^{12}}</annotation></semantics></math> at the start).
Each internal node has <math id="S4.p17.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n(n+1)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n(n+1)</annotation></semantics></math> children (leaf nodes) grouped into <math id="S4.p17.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> groups of <math id="S4.p17.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n+1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n+1</annotation></semantics></math> leaves each. We write <math id="S4.p17.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{i,j}=\{v_{i,j,0},v_{i,j,1},\dots,v_{i,j,n}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{i,j}=\{v_{i,j,0},v_{i,j,1},\dots,v_{i,j,n}\}</annotation></semantics></math> to denote the <math id="S4.p17.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>j</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j</annotation></semantics></math>-th group of children of the <math id="S4.p17.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>-th internal vertex.
All the leaves <math id="S4.p17.m17" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{i,j,k}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{i,j,k}</annotation></semantics></math> have initial size <math id="S4.p17.m18" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>. Thus, the vertices in the full graph in our construction consists of the root <math id="S4.p17.m19" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math>, internal nodes <math id="S4.p17.m20" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\{v_{0},v_{1},\dots,v_{n-1}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\{v_{0},v_{1},\dots,v_{n-1}\}</annotation></semantics></math> and leaves <math id="S4.p17.m21" class="ltx_math_unparsed" alttext="\cup_{i=0}^{n-1}\cup_{j=0}^{n-1}\cup_{k=0}^{n}\{v_{i,j,k}\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msubsup><mo>∪</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">∪</mo><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msubsup><msubsup><mo lspace="0em">∪</mo><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><mi>n</mi></msubsup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><msub><mi>v</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>k</mi></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\cup_{i=0}^{n-1}\cup_{j=0}^{n-1}\cup_{k=0}^{n}\{v_{i,j,k}\}</annotation></semantics></math></span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p18" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p18.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p18.1.1" class="ltx_text">Let <math id="S4.p18.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{i}=n^{8}+i\cdot n^{4}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>8</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{i}=n^{8}+i\cdot n^{4}</annotation></semantics></math> (for <math id="S4.p18.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0\leq i&lt;n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>≤</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>&lt;</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0\leq i&lt;n</annotation></semantics></math>).
For each pair of an internal node and each of its groups there are only two distinct edge weights for edges between the internal node and the leaves in the group.
Specifically, for an internal node <math id="S4.p18.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> and group <math id="S4.p18.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j,i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j,i}</annotation></semantics></math>
of its children we have <math id="S4.p18.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A[i,j]+1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A[i,j]+1</annotation></semantics></math> edges of weight <math id="S4.p18.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{r_{i}-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{r_{i}-1}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p18.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-A[i,j]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-A[i,j]</annotation></semantics></math> edges of weight <math id="S4.p18.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{r_{i}+i\cdot n^{3}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{r_{i}+i\cdot n^{3}}</annotation></semantics></math>. Specifically, we set</span></p>
</div>

</div>
<div id="S4.p20" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p20.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p20.1.1" class="ltx_text">We call the two weights <em id="S4.p20.1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">high-weight</em> and <em id="S4.p20.1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">low-weight</em> edges, respectively.
Note that their normalized weights are <math id="S4.p20.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{n^{4}(r_{i}-1)}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{n^{4}(r_{i}-1)}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p20.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{n^{4}(r_{i}+i\cdot n^{3})}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{n^{4}(r_{i}+i\cdot n^{3})}</annotation></semantics></math> respectively. Observe that the setting of <em id="S4.p20.1.1.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">high-weight</em> and <em id="S4.p20.1.1.4" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">low-weight</em> edges is independent of the internal node <math id="S4.p20.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>v</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v_{j}</annotation></semantics></math>, although the number of high versus low-weight edges depends on <math id="S4.p20.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A[i,j]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>j</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A[i,j]</annotation></semantics></math>.
Moreover, note that even low-weight edges of any group <math id="S4.p20.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j,i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j,i}</annotation></semantics></math> have higher weights than high-weight edges of group <math id="S4.p20.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j,i+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j,i+1}</annotation></semantics></math>:</span></p>

<p id="S4.p20.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p20.2.1" class="ltx_text">which follows from the fact that <math id="S4.p20.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i\leq n-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i\leq n-1</annotation></semantics></math>.
We now demonstrate that the average linkage HAC on this instance works in <math id="S4.p20.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>n</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math> phases numbered from <math id="S4.p20.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="S4.p20.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-1</annotation></semantics></math>, where in each phase <math id="S4.p20.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>,</span></p>
<ul id="S4.I2" class="ltx_itemize">
<li id="S4.I2.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="S4.I2.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I2.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><math id="S4.I2.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-1</annotation></semantics></math> internal nodes contract all of their incident group <math id="S4.I2.i1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> edges, and</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I2.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="S4.I2.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I2.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">one internal node contracts all of its high-weight edges to group <math id="S4.I2.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>, after which it merges with the root.</p>
</div></li>
</ul>
</div>
<div id="S4.p21" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p21.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p21.1.1" class="ltx_text">Because of the internal node merging with the root, the root has incident leaves, but they are connected with edges of (normalized) edge weights <math id="S4.p21.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\leq\frac{1}{n^{15}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>15</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\leq\frac{1}{n^{15}}</annotation></semantics></math> and so they will be irrelevant until all phases have been completed.
We show that if we denote by <math id="S4.p21.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> the index of the internal node which merges with the root in phase <math id="S4.p21.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>, the sequence <math id="S4.p21.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{0},\ldots,k_{n-1}</annotation></semantics></math> is a correct solution to the <span id="S4.p21.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> problem.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p22" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p22.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p22.1.1" class="ltx_text">In order to analyze the algorithm, we prove the following claim.
For convenience, let us define <math id="S4.p22.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{i}=n^{4}+i\cdot(n+1)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{i}=n^{4}+i\cdot(n+1)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="Thmclaim1" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_claim">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmclaim1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Claim 1</span></span><span id="Thmclaim1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmclaim1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmclaim1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmclaim1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">In the beginning of phase <math id="Thmclaim1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>, the graph is as follows:</span></p>
<ol id="S4.I3" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="S4.I3.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span> 
<div id="S4.I3.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I3.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.I3.i1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">The size of the root node is </span><math id="S4.I3.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r_{i}+i^{2}\cdot\Delta_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mrow><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r_{i}+i^{2}\cdot\Delta_{i}</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic"> for some </span><math id="S4.I3.i1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta_{i}\in[0,n+1]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta_{i}\in[0,n+1]</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i1.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">.</span></p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I3.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span> 
<div id="S4.I3.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I3.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.I3.i2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Exactly </span><math id="S4.I3.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i2.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic"> internal nodes have been merged with the root, and the corresponding values </span><math id="S4.I3.i2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{0},\ldots,k_{i-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>k</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>k</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{0},\ldots,k_{i-1}</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i2.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic"> have been computed correctly.</span></p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I3.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">3.</span> 
<div id="S4.I3.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I3.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.I3.i3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">The size of each of the </span><math id="S4.I3.i3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i3.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic"> remaining internal nodes is </span><math id="S4.I3.i3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{i}</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i3.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">.</span></p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I3.i4" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">4.</span> 
<div id="S4.I3.i4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I3.i4.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.I3.i4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">For all remaining internal nodes, </span><em id="S4.I3.i4.p1.1.2" class="ltx_emph">all</em><span id="S4.I3.i4.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic"> leaves in groups </span><math id="S4.I3.i4.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0,\ldots,i-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0,\ldots,i-1</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i4.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic"> have been merged into their parents, and no leaves in groups </span><math id="S4.I3.i4.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i,\ldots,n-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i,\ldots,n-1</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i4.p1.1.5" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic"> have been merged.</span></p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I3.i5" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">5.</span> 
<div id="S4.I3.i5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I3.i5.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.I3.i5.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">The root may have incident leaves (resulting from internal nodes contracting into it) connected to the root with edges of normalized weights </span><math id="S4.I3.i5.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\leq\frac{1}{n^{15}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≤</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>15</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\leq\frac{1}{n^{15}}</annotation></semantics></math><span id="S4.I3.i5.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">.</span></p>
</div></li>
</ol>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.3.1" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S4.p23" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p23.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p23.1.1" class="ltx_text">We prove the above claim using induction on <math id="S4.p23.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>.
The base case of <math id="S4.p23.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i=0</annotation></semantics></math> follows directly from how the tree is constructed.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p24" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p24.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p24.1.1" class="ltx_text">We now simulate a single phase.
The edges between the root and the internal nodes have (normalized) weights <math id="S4.p24.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{w_{i}(r_{i}+i^{2}\cdot\Delta_{i})}&gt;\frac{1}{n^{15}}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mrow><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><mo>&gt;</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>15</mn></msup></mfrac></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{w_{i}(r_{i}+i^{2}\cdot\Delta_{i})}&gt;\frac{1}{n^{15}}</annotation></semantics></math>.
Hence, the additional leaf nodes incident to the root (see <a href="#S4.I3.i5" title="In Claim 1. ‣ Proof. ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Item</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a> of the Claim) are irrelevant.
Thus, the highest weight edge in the graph is surely incident to one of the internal nodes.
Observe that the relative order of edge weights between an internal node <math id="S4.p24.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> and its children does not change as the leaves are merged into <math id="S4.p24.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math>.
Therefore, given that groups <math id="S4.p24.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0,\ldots,i-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0,\ldots,i-1</annotation></semantics></math> do not exist anymore, among edges between the internal nodes and leaves, the edges of group <math id="S4.p24.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> have the highest weights.
In the beginning of a phase the high-weight edges in that group have normalized weights <math id="S4.p24.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{w_{i}(r_{i}-1)}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{w_{i}(r_{i}-1)}</annotation></semantics></math> and the low-weight edges have weight <math id="S4.p24.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{w_{i}(r_{i}+i\cdot n^{2})}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{w_{i}(r_{i}+i\cdot n^{2})}</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p25" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p25.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p25.1.1" class="ltx_text">Hence, we have that if we sort the edges by their normalized weights, the top 3 classes of edges are, starting from the highest weight:</span></p>
<ol id="S4.I4" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="S4.I4.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span> 
<div id="S4.I4.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I4.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">High-weight edges between internal nodes and leaves of group <math id="S4.I4.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I4.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span> 
<div id="S4.I4.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I4.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Edges between the root and the internal nodes.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I4.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">3.</span> 
<div id="S4.I4.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I4.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Low-weight edges between internal nodes and leaves of group <math id="S4.I4.i3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div></li>
</ol>
</div>
<div id="S4.p26" class="ltx_para ltx_noindent">
<p id="S4.p26.1" class="ltx_p">We will show that the phase consists of the following sub-phases.</p>
<ol id="S4.I5" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="S4.I5.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span> 
<div id="S4.I5.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I5.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">First, there is some number of subphases, where each of <math id="S4.I5.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i</annotation></semantics></math> internal nodes contract one incident high-weight edge.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I5.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span> 
<div id="S4.I5.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I5.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Then, there is exactly one subphase, where <math id="S4.I5.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i-1</annotation></semantics></math> nodes contract an incident high-weight edge and one internal node merges with the root.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S4.I5.i3" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">3.</span> 
<div id="S4.I5.i3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.I5.i3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Then, the remaining <math id="S4.I5.i3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i-1</annotation></semantics></math> internal nodes merge with all of their group <math id="S4.I5.i3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> leaves (we do not analyze the order in this subphase, as it is irrelevant).</p>
</div></li>
</ol>
</div>
<div id="S4.p27" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p27.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p27.1.1" class="ltx_text">Assume that each internal node has at least one high-weight edge in group <math id="S4.p27.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>.
Then, the algorithm will execute a Type 1 subphase: the first <math id="S4.p27.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i</annotation></semantics></math> steps of the algorithm would merge exactly one high-weight edge incident to each internal node.
Note that when an edge incident to an internal node <math id="S4.p27.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> merges, the weight of <math id="S4.p27.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> increases, and so the incident edge weights decrease.
This guarantees that in the considered <math id="S4.p27.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i</annotation></semantics></math> steps exactly one merge per internal node happens.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p28" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p28.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p28.1.1" class="ltx_text">Type 1 subphases of <math id="S4.p28.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i</annotation></semantics></math> steps continue as long as each each internal node has at least one high-weight group <math id="S4.p28.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> edge in the beginning of the subphase.
Each Type 1 subphase also causes the weight of each internal node to increase by <math id="S4.p28.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>.
Clearly, since nodes are being merged into internal nodes, the ordering of edge weights incident to any internal node does not change.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p29" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p29.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p29.1.1" class="ltx_text">At some point, in the beginning of a subphase there is an internal node that does not have any incident high-weight edge in group <math id="S4.p29.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>.
Assume that this happened after <math id="S4.p29.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="p" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>p</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math> Type 1 subphases have completed.
Thus, the size of each internal node is <math id="S4.p29.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{i}+p" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{i}+p</annotation></semantics></math>.
Since by the construction each internal node had a different number of high-weight edges in group <math id="S4.p29.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>, there is exactly one node <math id="S4.p29.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> with no high-weight incident edges and that node merges with the root.
This is when Type 2 subphase happens.
First, <math id="S4.p29.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i-1</annotation></semantics></math> internal nodes contract with a high-weight incident group <math id="S4.p29.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> edge.
At this point the edge weights are as follows.
The weight of an edge between <math id="S4.p29.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="v" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>v</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">v</annotation></semantics></math> and the root is <math id="S4.p29.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{(w_{i}+p)(r_{i}+i^{2}\cdot\Delta_{i})}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mrow><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{(w_{i}+p)(r_{i}+i^{2}\cdot\Delta_{i})}</annotation></semantics></math> and the weight of a high-weight edge in group <math id="S4.p29.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> is
<math id="S4.p29.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\frac{1}{(w_{i}+p+1)(r_{i}-1)}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mi>p</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msub><mi>r</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{1}{(w_{i}+p+1)(r_{i}-1)}</annotation></semantics></math>. We have that the former is larger since</span></p>

<p id="S4.p29.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p29.2.1" class="ltx_text">Thus, the internal node with no incident high-weight edges in group <math id="S4.p29.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> merges with the root.
Observe that this is exactly the internal node which had the lowest number of high-weight edges in group <math id="S4.p29.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> among all remaining internal nodes.
This immediately implies that <math id="S4.p29.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> is computed correctly, proving <a href="#S4.I3.i2" title="In Claim 1. ‣ Proof. ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Item</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p30" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p30.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p30.1.1" class="ltx_text">We now show that in the remaining part of the phase the <math id="S4.p30.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-i-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-i-1</annotation></semantics></math> remaining internal nodes contract their incident group <math id="S4.p30.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> edges.
First, observe that the new size of the root node is</span></p>

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">

<p id="S4.p30.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p30.2.1" class="ltx_text">for some <math id="S4.p30.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta_{i+1}\in[0,n+1]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta_{i+1}\in[0,n+1]</annotation></semantics></math>.
Note that we use the fact that both <math id="S4.p30.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Delta_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Delta_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S4.p30.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="p" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>p</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math> are upper bounded by <math id="S4.p30.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n+1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n+1</annotation></semantics></math>, which implies <math id="S4.p30.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i\cdot(n+1)+i^{2}\cdot\Delta_{i}+p\leq(i+1)^{2}(n+1)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><msup><mi>i</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="normal">Δ</mi><mi>i</mi></msub></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow><mo>≤</mo><mrow><msup><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i\cdot(n+1)+i^{2}\cdot\Delta_{i}+p\leq(i+1)^{2}(n+1)</annotation></semantics></math>.
This proves <a href="#S4.I3.i1" title="In Claim 1. ‣ Proof. ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Item</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>.
Thus for an internal node of weight <math id="S4.p30.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>w</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w</annotation></semantics></math>, the weight of its edge to the root is</span></p>
