<p id="S4.p30.4" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p30.4.1" class="ltx_text">As a result, in the remaining part of the current phase all internal nodes will contract all their incident group <math id="S4.p30.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> edges. This implies <a href="#S4.I3.i4" title="In Claim 1. ‣ Proof. ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Item</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>.
Thus, the size of each internal node within the phase increases to <math id="S4.p30.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w_{i}+(n+1)=n^{4}+i\cdot(n+1)+n+1=n^{4}+(i+1)(n+1)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo>+</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo lspace="0.222em" rspace="0.222em">⋅</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>4</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w_{i}+(n+1)=n^{4}+i\cdot(n+1)+n+1=n^{4}+(i+1)(n+1)</annotation></semantics></math>, as required.
This proves <a href="#S4.I3.i3" title="In Claim 1. ‣ Proof. ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Item</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> and completes the proof.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S4.p31" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p31.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p31.1.1" class="ltx_text">Finally, we now claim that, given an instance of <span id="S4.p31.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Adaptive Minimum</span> with input index <math id="S4.p31.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x\in[0,n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x\in[0,n)</annotation></semantics></math> and an algorithm which can compute the solutions to Problem <a href="#Thmproblem2" title="Problem 2 (Average Linkage HAC). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>, we can compute the solution <math id="S4.p31.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{x}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{x}</annotation></semantics></math> to Problem <a href="#Thmproblem3" title="Problem 3 (Adaptive Minimum). ‣ 4 Average Linkage HAC is Hard to Parallelize Even on Trees ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> in logspace. To see this, note that it suffices to determine the value <math id="S4.p31.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{x}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{x}</annotation></semantics></math> as defined above given an algorithm for <span id="S4.p31.1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Average Linkage HAC</span>. To see this, note that for any internal node <math id="S4.p31.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math>, we can query the <span id="S4.p31.1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_sansserif">Average Linkage HAC</span> algorithm to determine which time step <math id="S4.p31.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> it merged with the root.
This does not directly tell us which phase <math id="S4.p31.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> merged with the root, but for a given <math id="S4.p31.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> we can determine if it merged in phase <math id="S4.p31.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math>
by comparing <math id="S4.p31.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t_{i}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S4.p31.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> for all <math id="S4.p31.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j\in\{0,1,\dots,n-1\}\setminus\{i\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><mo>∖</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j\in\{0,1,\dots,n-1\}\setminus\{i\}</annotation></semantics></math>, and checking if there are exactly <math id="S4.p31.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x-1</annotation></semantics></math> values of <math id="S4.p31.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> smaller than <math id="S4.p31.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>t</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>. This clearly can be verified in log-space.
Repeating for all <math id="S4.p31.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i\in\{0,1,\dots,n-1\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i\in\{0,1,\dots,n-1\}</annotation></semantics></math> allows us to correctly determine the identity of the internal node that merged with the root in phase <math id="S4.p31.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>x</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x</annotation></semantics></math>, and therefore the value of <math id="S4.p31.m17" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k_{x}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>k</mi><mi>x</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k_{x}</annotation></semantics></math>, in logspace as required.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S4.p32" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S4.p32.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S4.p32.1.1" class="ltx_text">To complete the proof of the Lemma it remains to show how to drop the assumption on the node sizes being initially not all equal to <math id="S4.p32.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>.
In order to obtain a node of size <math id="S4.p32.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>w</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w</annotation></semantics></math> it suffices to create a node of weight <math id="S4.p32.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math> and initially connect it to <math id="S4.p32.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w-1</annotation></semantics></math> auxiliary nodes using very high weight edges.
This will force the algorithm to merge all these auxiliary nodes and increase the size of that node to <math id="S4.p32.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="w" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>w</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">w</annotation></semantics></math>.
Since the auxiliary leaves are connected only to the root and internal nodes (the leaves in our construction have weight <math id="S4.p32.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>), the diameter of the tree does not increase.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S5" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">5 </span>Average Linkage HAC on Paths in <span id="S5.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">NC</span></h2>

<div id="S5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In this section, we present an <math id="S5.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{O}(n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>O</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{O}(n)</annotation></semantics></math> work and <math id="S5.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\mathsf{polylog}(n))" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗈𝗅𝗒𝗅𝗈𝗀</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\mathsf{polylog}(n))</annotation></semantics></math> depth algorithm for solving average linkage HAC on path graphs, provided that the aspect ratio of the input instance is bounded by <math id="S5.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{poly}(n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗈𝗅𝗒</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{poly}(n)</annotation></semantics></math>.
The <span id="S5.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">aspect ratio</span> is defined as <math id="S5.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{A}=W_{\max}/W_{\min}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒜</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>/</mo><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>min</mi></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{A}=W_{\max}/W_{\min}</annotation></semantics></math>, where <math id="S5.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="W_{\max}=\argmax_{e\in E}w(e)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>max</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>arg</mi><mo lspace="0.170em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>max</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>E</mi></mrow></msub><mo>⁡</mo><mi>w</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>e</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{\max}=\argmax_{e\in E}w(e)</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S5.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="W_{\min}=\argmin_{e\in E}w(e)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><msub><mrow><mi>arg</mi><mo lspace="0.170em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>min</mi></mrow><mrow><mi>e</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>E</mi></mrow></msub><mo>⁡</mo><mi>w</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>e</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{\min}=\argmin_{e\in E}w(e)</annotation></semantics></math> (note that this definition excludes all non-edges, which implicitly have a weight of 0).
The main algorithm for this section is presented in Algorithm <a href="#alg3" title="Algorithm 3 ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>, which we first give a high-level overview of next, followed by a detailed description.</p>
</div>

<br class="ltx_break"><span id="alg3.3.4.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">for</span> <em id="alg3.3.4.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic"><math id="alg3.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="t=1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>t</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">t=1</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="alg3.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="T" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>T</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T</annotation></semantics></math></em> <span id="alg3.3.4.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">do</span>

<div id="alg3.3.7" class="ltx_listingline">
<span id="alg3.3.7.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Function</span> <em id="alg3.3.7.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_typewriter">ProcessChain(<em id="alg3.3.7.2.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_serif ltx_font_italic"><math id="alg3.m18" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C,t" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>t</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C,t</annotation></semantics></math></em>)</em><span id="alg3.3.7.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">:</span>

</div>
<div id="alg3.3.8" class="ltx_listingline">
Split the chain <math id="alg3.m19" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math> at all indices <math id="alg3.m20" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>i</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i</annotation></semantics></math> such that <math id="alg3.m21" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S(c_{i})&lt;2S(c_{i-1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S(c_{i})&lt;2S(c_{i-1})</annotation></semantics></math>, except <math id="alg3.m22" class="ltx_Math" alttext="i=2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">i=2</annotation></semantics></math>.
<br class="ltx_break">We get a set of contiguous <em id="alg3.3.8.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">subchains</em> <math id="alg3.m23" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{K}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>K</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{K}</annotation></semantics></math>. Subchain <math id="alg3.m24" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> falls into one of two categories:
(A) the nearest clusters in <math id="alg3.m25" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j-1}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="alg3.m26" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> merge, or
(B) no two clusters from <math id="alg3.m27" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j-1}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="alg3.m28" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> merge.
<br class="ltx_break">Find the merge categories for all subchains to obtain the modified subchains <math id="alg3.m29" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,C_{K}^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>′</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,C_{K}^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>.
<br class="ltx_break"><span id="alg3.3.8.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">parallel for</span> <em id="alg3.3.8.3" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">each <math id="alg3.m30" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j=1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j=1</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="alg3.m31" class="ltx_Math" alttext="k" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>k</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math></em><span id="alg3.3.8.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">:</span>
            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline">6</span>
          
</div>
<div id="alg3.3.9" class="ltx_listingline"> <span class="ltx_rule" style="width:1px;height:100%;--ltx-bg-color:black;display:inline-block;"> </span> 
Run average linkage HAC on <math id="alg3.m32" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j}^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mi>j</mi><mo>′</mo></msubsup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j}^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> until no edge remains with weight within the threshold of <math id="alg3.m33" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B_{t}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B_{t}</annotation></semantics></math>.

            <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline">7</span>
          
</div>
<div id="alg3.3.10" class="ltx_listingline"> <span class="ltx_rule" style="width:1px;height:100%;--ltx-bg-color:black;display:inline-block;"> </span> 
</div>
</div>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_float"><span id="alg3.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Algorithm 3</span> </span>Average linkage HAC on paths; <math id="alg3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathtt{ProcessChain}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝙿𝚛𝚘𝚌𝚎𝚜𝚜𝙲𝚑𝚊𝚒𝚗</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathtt{ProcessChain}</annotation></semantics></math> assumes that the input chain has a certain structure. However, this assumption can be removed easily (see <a href="#S5" title="5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Section</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>)</figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S5.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p2.1" class="ltx_p">In average linkage HAC, the weight (i.e., similarity) of edges monotonically decreases over time.
Thus, our idea is to partition the edges into <em id="S5.p2.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">buckets</em> where the edges in any bucket have the same similarity, up to constant factors.
Next, we process these buckets in <span id="S5.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">phases</span>, from the highest similarity bucket to the lowest.
In each phase, we perform a modified version of the classic nearest-neighbor chain algorithm (<a href="#alg4" title="In Height of the dendrogram is 
          
            
              O
              
                
                
                  (
                  
                    
                      
                      log
                      2
                    
                    n
                  
                  ) ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Algorithm</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>), wherein we compute the nearest-neighbor chains for the graph induced on the edges in that bucket, and process each chain independently.
We note that when we use the terminology <span id="S5.p2.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">nearest neighbor</span> of a vertex in what follows, we refer to the neighbor along the <span id="S5.p2.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">highest weight edge</span> incident to the vertex.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.p3.1" class="ltx_p">Initially, each cluster is a singleton, and we might end up with <math id="S5.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\Omega(n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Ω</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\Omega(n)</annotation></semantics></math> sequential dependencies to resolve.
However, we observe that in this special case when the size of every cluster is equal, starting with the reciprocal pair, every <span id="S5.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">alternate</span> edge in this chain can be merged independently, and the rest of the edges will be moved to a later bucket.
We can compute the edges that will be merged easily via a simple <math id="S5.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗋𝖾𝖿𝗂𝗑</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mtext>-</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>𝗌𝗎𝗆</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}</annotation></semantics></math> routine <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib7" title="" class="ltx_ref">7</a>]</cite>.
However, when the cluster sizes are arbitrary, this observation no longer holds.
Nonetheless, we show that we can partition each chain further into <math id="S5.p3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>-sized subchains such that, even though the dependencies within a subchain must be resolved sequentially, the dependencies across subchains can be resolved in parallel using a similar application of <math id="S5.p3.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗋𝖾𝖿𝗂𝗑</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mtext>-</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>𝗌𝗎𝗆</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}</annotation></semantics></math>.
In this section we show (1) that our parallel algorithm is highly efficient (it runs near-linear time in the number of nodes) and runs in poly-logarithmic depth and (2) that our algorithm implies that the dendrogram height of a path input with polynomial aspect ratio is always poly-logarithmic.</p>
</div>
<section id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Implementation Details and Correctness</h4>

<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We now elaborate on certain implementation aspects of <a href="#alg3" title="In 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Algorithm</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>. In <a href="#alg3" title="In 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Algorithm</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>, there are two implicit assumptions made about the chains:</p>
<ul id="S5.I1" class="ltx_itemize">
<li id="S5.I1.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="S5.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.I1.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Every edge in <math id="S5.I1.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B_{t}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B_{t}</annotation></semantics></math> is present in some chain in <math id="S5.I1.i1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{C}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒞</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{C}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S5.I1.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">•</span> 
<div id="S5.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.I1.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Each chain can be represented as <math id="S5.I1.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{N})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>N</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{N})</annotation></semantics></math> such that <math id="S5.I1.i2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{i-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>c</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{i-1}</annotation></semantics></math> is the nearest neighbor of cluster <math id="S5.I1.i2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>, and <math id="S5.I1.i2.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(c_{1},c_{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(c_{1},c_{2})</annotation></semantics></math> is the <em id="S5.I1.i2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">reciprocal pair</em>.
Thus, the weights are in non-increasing order from left to right.</p>
</div></li>
</ul>
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.2" class="ltx_p">We will see later how to remove these assumptions. By the above assumptions, each chain will be a separate connected component in the graph induced on <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>B</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>, thus, allowing us to process each chain independently. In <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathtt{ProcessChain}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝙿𝚛𝚘𝚌𝚎𝚜𝚜𝙲𝚑𝚊𝚒𝚗</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathtt{ProcessChain}</annotation></semantics></math>, we split each chain at all indices such that <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S(c_{i})&lt;2S(c_{i-1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>&lt;</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S(c_{i})&lt;2S(c_{i-1})</annotation></semantics></math> giving us a set of subchains. This implies that for two adjacent clusters <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{j-1},c_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>c</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>j</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{j-1},c_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> in the same subchain, <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S(c_{j})\geq 2S_{j-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S(c_{j})\geq 2S_{j-1}</annotation></semantics></math> (see <a href="#S5.F6" title="In Implementation Details and Correctness ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>). Thus, the size of a subchain cannot exceed <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\log n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\log n</annotation></semantics></math>, and we can afford to process these subchains sequentially, while still obtaining low depth overall.</p>
</div>
<figure id="S5.F6" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="/html/2404.14730/assets/pathSubchains.svg" id="S5.F6.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="429" height="48"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S5.F6.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 6</span>: </span><span id="S5.F6.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">An example describing the partition of a chain into subchains based on cluster sizes.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p2.1" class="ltx_p">We now prove certain key properties about subchains. Let <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{0},u_{1},u_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{0},u_{1},u_{2}</annotation></semantics></math> denote three contiguous clusters in a chain such that <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{0}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{0}</annotation></semantics></math> is the last cluster in its subchain, and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> is the first cluster of its subchain. <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{2}</annotation></semantics></math> may or may not belong to the same subchain as <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p2.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{1}</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmlemma3" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmlemma3.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma 3</span></span><span id="Thmlemma3.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmlemma3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmlemma3.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmlemma3.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">If cluster <math id="Thmlemma3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> first merges with the cluster (containing) <math id="Thmlemma3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{0}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{0}</annotation></semantics></math>, then the weight of the edge <math id="Thmlemma3.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(u_{1},u_{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(u_{1},u_{2})</annotation></semantics></math> reduces to at most <math id="Thmlemma3.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2/3" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2/3</annotation></semantics></math> times its previous weight.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.1" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text">Let <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x=w(e)/(S(u_{1})S(u_{2}))" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>x</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>w</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>e</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x=w(e)/(S(u_{1})S(u_{2}))</annotation></semantics></math> denote the weight of edge <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e=(u_{1},u_{2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>e</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e=(u_{1},u_{2})</annotation></semantics></math>. If cluster <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> merges with a cluster in its preceding subchain, the merged cluster will be of size at least <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S(u_{0})+S(u_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S(u_{0})+S(u_{1})</annotation></semantics></math>. Let <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math> denote the new weight of edge <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="e" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>e</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e</annotation></semantics></math> after this merge. Then,</span></p>
<table id="S5.EGx4" class="ltx_equationgroup ltx_eqn_align ltx_eqn_table">

<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_left ltx_eqn_cell"><math id="S5.Ex14.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{x}+\frac{S(u_{1})S(u_{2})}{2w(e)}," display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mphantom></mphantom><mo>≥</mo><mrow><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>x</mi></mfrac></mstyle><mo>+</mo><mstyle displaystyle="true"><mfrac><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>S</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>w</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>e</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mfrac></mstyle></mrow></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{x}+\frac{S(u_{1})S(u_{2})}{2w(e)},</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_td ltx_align_right ltx_eqn_cell"><span id="S5.Ex14.4.1" class="ltx_text ltx_markedasmath">since <math id="S5.Ex14.m3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="S(u_{0})\geq S(u_{1})/2" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>S</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S(u_{0})\geq S(u_{1})/2</annotation></semantics></math></span></td>

</table>
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.2.1" class="ltx_text">Therefore, <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p3.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="x^{\prime}\leq 2x/3" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>x</mi></mrow><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^{\prime}\leq 2x/3</annotation></semantics></math>.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="Thmlemma4" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmlemma4.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma 4</span></span><span id="Thmlemma4.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmlemma4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmlemma4.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmlemma4.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">If cluster <math id="Thmlemma4.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> first merges with the cluster (containing) <math id="Thmlemma4.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u_{2}</annotation></semantics></math>, then the weight of the edge <math id="Thmlemma4.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(u_{0},u_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>u</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(u_{0},u_{1})</annotation></semantics></math> reduces to at most <math id="Thmlemma4.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1/3" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1/3</annotation></semantics></math> times its previous weight.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p4.1" class="ltx_p">The proof follows by a similar argument as in <a href="#Thmlemma3" title="Lemma 3. ‣ Implementation Details and Correctness ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Lemma</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>. By <a href="#Thmlemma3" title="Lemma 3. ‣ Implementation Details and Correctness ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Lemmas</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a> and <a href="#Thmlemma4" title="Lemma 4. ‣ Implementation Details and Correctness ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>, each subchain falls into one of the following categories:</p>
<ol id="S5.I2" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="S5.I2.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">(A)</span> 
<div id="S5.I2.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.I2.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The first cluster of the subchain merges with a cluster in the preceding subchain (if exists).</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S5.I2.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">(B)</span> 
<div id="S5.I2.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.I2.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The first cluster of the subchain doesn’t merge with a cluster from the preceding subchain.</p>
</div></li>
</ol>
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p4.2" class="ltx_p">In either case, one of the edges incident on the first cluster of the subchain moves to a later bucket after it merges, disconnecting the two subchains in the induced graph. Thus, if a subchain falls into category (A), we can move its first cluster to the preceding subchain; if it falls into category (B), we make no changes. By this process, we obtained the modified subchains <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,C_{K}^{\prime}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>C</mi><mi>K</mi><mo>′</mo></msubsup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{1}^{\prime},C_{2}^{\prime},\ldots,C_{K}^{\prime}</annotation></semantics></math>, and by the above argument we can run average linkage HAC independently on them. Note that we do not run HAC to completion at these subchains, rather until we have edges with weight within the threshold defined by its bucket.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p5.1" class="ltx_p">Note that the first subchain of a chain will always fall into category (B) since it contains a reciprocal pair which is guaranteed to merge.
Running HAC on this subchain (starting with merging the reciprocal pair) determines whether the next subchain falls into the merge category (A) or (B), which in turn determines the merge category for the following subchain, and so on.
We now show that we can propagate this merge behavior across subchains with the help of a simple prefix-sum routine in <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{poly}(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗈𝗅𝗒</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{poly}(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> depth.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmlemma5" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmlemma5.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma 5</span></span><span id="Thmlemma5.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmlemma5.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmlemma5.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmlemma5.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Given a chain of size <math id="Thmlemma5.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>N</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math> partitioned into subchains <math id="Thmlemma5.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{K}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>K</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{1},C_{2},\ldots,C_{K}</annotation></semantics></math>, we can determine the merge category ((A) or (B)) for each subchain in <math id="Thmlemma5.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{O}(N)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>O</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>N</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{O}(N)</annotation></semantics></math> work and <math id="Thmlemma5.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{poly}(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗈𝗅𝗒</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{poly}(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> depth.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.2" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.1.1" class="ltx_text">For subchain <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j}</annotation></semantics></math>, let <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(j,A)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(j,A)</annotation></semantics></math> denote the resulting merge category of <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j+1}</annotation></semantics></math> if <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> belongs to category (A). Define <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(j,B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(j,B)</annotation></semantics></math> similarly. Firstly, recall that the category of <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> is determined (it is always category (B)).
If <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(j,A)=f(j,B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(j,A)=f(j,B)</annotation></semantics></math>, for some <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>j</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j</annotation></semantics></math>, this implies that the category of <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j+1}</annotation></semantics></math> is determined regardless of the category of <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j}</annotation></semantics></math>. Thus, we can split the chain at subchain <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j+1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mrow><mi>j</mi><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j+1}</annotation></semantics></math> and deal with the two chains obtained, independently. We now assume that <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(j,A)\neq f(j,B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≠</mo><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(j,A)\neq f(j,B)</annotation></semantics></math> for all <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p6.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="j" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>j</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">j</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.1.1" class="ltx_text">We create an array <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> of size <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="K" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>K</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">K</annotation></semantics></math> such that,</span></p>

<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.2" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.2.1" class="ltx_text">Let <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B=\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}(A,\mathtt{XOR},0)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>B</mi><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗋𝖾𝖿𝗂𝗑</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mtext>-</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>𝗌𝗎𝗆</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>𝚇𝙾𝚁</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B=\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}(A,\mathtt{XOR},0)</annotation></semantics></math>, i.e., <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B[1]=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B[1]=0</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B[i]=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}\oplus\ldots A_{i-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>i</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>⊕</mo><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>⊕</mo><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><msub><mi>A</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B[i]=A_{1}\oplus A_{2}\oplus\ldots A_{i-1}</annotation></semantics></math>.
In other words, we compute the prefix sum of the array <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> with respect to the <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathtt{XOR}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝚇𝙾𝚁</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathtt{XOR}</annotation></semantics></math> function, using <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0</annotation></semantics></math> as the left identity element.
Then, we claim that the merge category of subchain <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{j}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{j}</annotation></semantics></math> is (A) if <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p7.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B[j]=0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>B</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>j</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mn>0</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B[j]=0</annotation></semantics></math>, otherwise it is (B).</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.1.1" class="ltx_text">To see this, consider subchains such that <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(j,A)=A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi>A</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(j,A)=A</annotation></semantics></math>. These subchains propagate the same category (as them) to the next subchain, which can be viewed as an <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathtt{XOR}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝚇𝙾𝚁</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathtt{XOR}</annotation></semantics></math> operation with a <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="0" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>0</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0</annotation></semantics></math>. Similarly, subchains that have <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(j,A)=B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(j,A)=B</annotation></semantics></math> propagate the opposite category (as them) to the next subchain, which can be viewed as an <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathtt{XOR}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>𝚇𝙾𝚁</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathtt{XOR}</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mn>1</mn><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math>. <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A[1]" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A[1]</annotation></semantics></math> denotes the category of subchain <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{2}</annotation></semantics></math> that is determined by <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>C</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C_{1}</annotation></semantics></math>. Thus, computing the <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p8.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗋𝖾𝖿𝗂𝗑</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mtext>-</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>𝗌𝗎𝗆</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}</annotation></semantics></math> correctly computes the categories for each subchain.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p9" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p9.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p9.1.1" class="ltx_text">The values of <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p9.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(j,A)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(j,A)</annotation></semantics></math> (and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p9.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="f(j,B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>j</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f(j,B)</annotation></semantics></math>) can be computed by simply simulating average linkage HAC sequentially at each subchain, assuming the category (A) (and (B)) at that subchain.
Since the subchain sizes are at most <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p9.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\log n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\log n</annotation></semantics></math>, the work incurred to solve HAC on a subchain of size <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p9.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math> will be <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p9.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(r\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>log</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(r\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.1.1" class="ltx_text">To see this, consider running Algorithm <a href="#alg5" title="Algorithm 5 ‣ Nearest-Neighbor Chain Analysis ‣ 6 
        
          
            O

       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a> on the chain, which we claim runs in <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(N\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>log</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(N\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> work (and depth).
This is because the heap-based algorithm for HAC (described in more detail in Section <a href="#S6" title="6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
