                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>) on a path containing <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r\leq\log n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo>≤</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r\leq\log n</annotation></semantics></math> elements operates over a heap containing at most <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\log n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\log n</annotation></semantics></math> elements, and thus each of the at most <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="r" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>r</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">r</annotation></semantics></math> merge operations require <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> time and only require updating the two neighboring edges incident to the merged edge.
Since the algorithm used on each subchain is sequential, the depth at each subchain will also be <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(r\log\log n)\in O(\log n\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>r</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(r\log\log n)\in O(\log n\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.
The <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗋𝖾𝖿𝗂𝗑</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mtext>-</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>𝗌𝗎𝗆</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{prefix\text{-}sum}</annotation></semantics></math> operation runs in <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(K)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>K</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(K)</annotation></semantics></math> work and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p10.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log K)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>K</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log K)</annotation></semantics></math> depth.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p11" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p11.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p11.1.1" class="ltx_text">Thus, the overall work for a chain containing <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p11.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>N</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math> elements will be <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p11.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(N\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>log</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(N\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> and depth will be <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p11.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>log</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p12" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p12.1" class="ltx_p">Since the edges are processed in non-increasing order of weight (due to bucketing), and <a href="#Thmlemma3" title="Lemma 3. ‣ Implementation Details and Correctness ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Lemmas</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>, <a href="#Thmlemma4" title="Lemma 4. ‣ Implementation Details and Correctness ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> and <a href="#Thmlemma5" title="Lemma 5. ‣ Implementation Details and Correctness ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a> correctly computes the subchains and proves the correctness of processing these subchains independently, the overall correctness of the algorithm follows directly.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Work and Depth Bounds</h4>

<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We will now argue the work and depth bounds of the overall algorithm.
Observe that, since the minimum possible weight is at least <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="W_{\min}/n^{2}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>W</mi><mi>min</mi></msub><mo>/</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">W_{\min}/n^{2}</annotation></semantics></math>, the total number of phases will be <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="T\in O(\log\mathcal{A}+\log n)\in O(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">𝒜</mi></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">T\in O(\log\mathcal{A}+\log n)\in O(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.
In each phase, we can compute the nearest neighbor chains in <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n)</annotation></semantics></math> work and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> depth; the subchains can also be computed with similar work-depth bounds. By <a href="#Thmlemma5" title="Lemma 5. ‣ Implementation Details and Correctness ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Lemma</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>, the total work incurred in finding the merge categories for each subchain and running average linkage HAC on these subchains, across all chains, in the worst case will be <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>log</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> work and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>log</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> depth. Thus, the overall work of the algorithm is <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n\log n\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>log</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n\log n\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> and depth is <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log^{2}n\log\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mrow><msup><mi>log</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log^{2}n\log\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">Thus, we have the following theorem about the work and depth of the algorithm:
See <a href="#Thmtheorem4" title="Theorem 4. ‣ 1.1 Our Contributions ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a></p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Resolving assumptions about chain structure</h4>

<figure id="S5.F7" class="ltx_figure"><object type="image/svg+xml" data="/html/2404.14730/assets/pathGeneralChains.svg" id="S5.F7.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_centering ltx_img_landscape" width="476" height="44"></object>
<figcaption class="ltx_caption ltx_centering"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_figure"><span id="S5.F7.3" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Figure 7</span>: </span><span id="S5.F7.4" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">An example illustrating the general (possible) structure of the nearest neighbor chains computed in each phase of <a href="#alg3" title="In 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Algorithm</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>. The red edge here is an example of an edge that is not present in any chain. The dotted lines represents the various subchain merges to address the assumptions made about the nearest neighbor chain structure in <a href="#alg3" title="In 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Algorithm</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We will now address the assumptions made about the structure of chains in the algorithm. When the nearest neighbor chains are computed, the structure of the chains can be represented as</p>
<table id="S5.Ex17" class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_table">

<tbody><tr class="ltx_equation ltx_eqn_row ltx_align_baseline">
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padleft"></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_align_center"><math id="S5.Ex17.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(c^{\prime}_{N^{\prime}}\ldots,c_{3}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime},c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},\ldots,c_{N})," display="block" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msubsup><mi>c</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi></mrow><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>3</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>N</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(c^{\prime}_{N^{\prime}}\ldots,c_{3}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},c_{1}^{\prime},c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},\ldots,c_{N}),</annotation></semantics></math></td>
<td class="ltx_eqn_cell ltx_eqn_center_padright"></td></tr></tbody>
</table>
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.2" class="ltx_p">where <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(c_{1}^{\prime},c_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(c_{1}^{\prime},c_{1})</annotation></semantics></math> is the reciprocal pair, <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c^{\prime}_{i-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><mo>′</mo></msubsup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c^{\prime}_{i-1}</annotation></semantics></math> is the nearest neighbor of <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c^{\prime}_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi><mo>′</mo></msubsup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c^{\prime}_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>, and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{i-1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>c</mi><mrow><mi>i</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{i-1}</annotation></semantics></math> is the nearest neighbor of <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{i}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{i}</annotation></semantics></math>. We can extend the idea of partitioning these chains into subchains as follows: partition the chains <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{N})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mi>N</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(c_{1},c_{2},\ldots,c_{N})</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,c^{\prime}_{N^{\prime}})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mn>2</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo>,</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">…</mi><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>c</mi><msup><mi>N</mi><mo>′</mo></msup><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(c^{\prime}_{1},c^{\prime}_{2},\ldots,c^{\prime}_{N^{\prime}})</annotation></semantics></math>, independently, into subchains using the same criterion as before. Now, merge the subchains containing <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="c^{\prime}_{1}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">c^{\prime}_{1}</annotation></semantics></math> resulting in a subchain of length at most <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2\log n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2\log n</annotation></semantics></math> (see <a href="#S5.F7" title="In Resolving assumptions about chain structure ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">7</span></a>). Then, independently propagate the merge behavior across these two sets of subchains, with the subchain containing <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(c_{1},c^{\prime}_{1})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn></msub><mo>,</mo><msubsup><mi>c</mi><mn>1</mn><mo>′</mo></msubsup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(c_{1},c^{\prime}_{1})</annotation></semantics></math> as the first subchain.</p>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.1" class="ltx_p">Finally, when we compute the nearest neighbor chains, it is possible for some edges in the bucket to not be present in any chain. However, these edges can occur only between the last vertices of two chains (see <a href="#S5.F7" title="In Resolving assumptions about chain structure ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">Figure</span> <span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">7</span></a>). Thus, after finding the merge categories for the subchains containing these vertices (in their respective chains), we can merge these two subchains along with the edge (whose size will be at most <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="4\log n" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4\log n</annotation></semantics></math> in the worst case). It is not hard to extend the correctness and running time arguments when these modifications are incorporated.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Height of the dendrogram is <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log^{2}n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝑶</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo class="ltx_mathvariant_bold" mathvariant="bold" stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msup><mi>𝐥𝐨𝐠</mi><mn>𝟐</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>𝒏</mi></mrow><mo class="ltx_mathvariant_bold" mathvariant="bold" stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log^{2}n)</annotation></semantics></math></h4>

<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">An interesting consequence of our algorithm is a constructive proof that the height of the dendrogram obtained when we run average linkage HAC on paths with polynomial aspect ratio is <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log^{2}n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msup><mi>log</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log^{2}n)</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmtheorem9" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_theorem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmtheorem9.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Theorem 9</span></span><span id="Thmtheorem9.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmtheorem9.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmtheorem9.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmtheorem9.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Average linkage HAC on path graphs with <math id="Thmtheorem9.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathsf{poly}(n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝗉𝗈𝗅𝗒</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathsf{poly}(n)</annotation></semantics></math> aspect-ratio returns a dendrogram with height at most <math id="Thmtheorem9.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log^{2}n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msup><mi>log</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log^{2}n)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.1" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p2.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text">Starting with phase 1, the algorithm runs average linkage HAC (partially) on independent <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>-sized contiguous portions (i.e., subchains) of the path. Thus, each subchain will correspond to independent parts in the output dendrogram. Since there are <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> phases and the max-height generated in each phase is at most <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>, we get an overall bound of <math id="S5.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log^{2}n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><msup><mi>log</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log^{2}n)</annotation></semantics></math> for the height in the worst case.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>

<section id="S6" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">6 </span><math id="S6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(mh\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>𝑶</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo class="ltx_mathvariant_bold" mathvariant="bold" stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>𝒎</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>𝒉</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>𝐥𝐨𝐠</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>𝒏</mi></mrow></mrow><mo class="ltx_mathvariant_bold" mathvariant="bold" stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(mh\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> Upper Bound</h2>

<div id="S6.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In this section we show that we can solve graph-based average linkage HAC in <math id="S6.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\tilde{O}(mh)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mover accent="true"><mi>O</mi><mo>~</mo></mover><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>h</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\tilde{O}(mh)</annotation></semantics></math> time where <math id="S6.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="h" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>h</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h</annotation></semantics></math> is the <span id="S6.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">height</span> of the output dendrogram.
Thus, when <math id="S6.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="mh=o(n^{3/2})" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>h</mi></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mi>o</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msup><mi>n</mi><mrow><mn>3</mn><mo>/</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow></msup><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">mh=o(n^{3/2})</annotation></semantics></math> this algorithm improves over the lower bound from Section <a href="#S3" title="3 An 
        
          
            Ω
            
              
              
                (
                
                  
                  n
                  
                    -
                    
                      /
                      3
                      2
                    
                    ϵ
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Conditional Lower Bound for Average Linkage HAC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">3</span></a>.
Interestingly, we give a simple analysis showing that either of the two classic approaches to HAC—the nearest-neighbor chain or heap-based algorithms—achieves this bound.
We give the pseudocode for the nearest-neighbor chain algorithm in Algorithm <a href="#alg4" title="Algorithm 4 ‣ Height of the dendrogram is 
          
            
              O
              
                
                
                  (
                  
                    
                      
                      log
                      2
                    
                    n
                  
                  ) ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a> and the heap-based algorithm in Algorithm <a href="#alg5" title="Algorithm 5 ‣ Nearest-Neighbor Chain Analysis ‣ 6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
                  
                    log
                    n
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>.
In a nutshell, in both algorithms initially all vertices start in their own cluster, and are <em id="S6.p1.1.2" class="ltx_emph ltx_font_italic">active</em>.
As the algorithms proceed, they find edges to merge that the exact greedy HAC algorithm (Algorithm <a href="#alg1" title="Algorithm 1 ‣ 2 Preliminaries ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">1</span></a>) will make and merge them, marking one of the endpoints as <span id="S6.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">inactive</span>.
This process continues until no further edges between active clusters remain.
We discuss the algorithms individually in more detail in what follows.</p>
</div>
<div id="S6.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.p2.1" class="ltx_p">We start by discussing some data structures and details in how the algorithms carry out merges.</p>
</div>
<section id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Neighborhood representation</h4>

<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1" class="ltx_p">The neighbors of each vertex are stored in a max-heap supporting standard operations (e.g., <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">find-min</span>, <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">delete-min</span>, <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">insert</span>, <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.1.4" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">decrease-key</span>).
The priority of a neighbor is the average linkage weight of the edge to the neighbor.
At the start of the algorithm, the heaps are initialized with the initial neighbors of each vertex.
Let <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(A)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(A)</annotation></semantics></math> represent the neighbors of a cluster <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math>.
For concreteness in what follows, we use Fibonacci heaps <cite class="ltx_cite ltx_citemacro_cite">[<a href="#bib.bib14" title="" class="ltx_ref">14</a>, <a href="#bib.bib19" title="" class="ltx_ref">19</a>]</cite>.</p>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Merging clusters</h4>

<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1" class="ltx_p">Next, we discuss how to implement the <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">merge</span> step in both algorithms, which merges two clusters <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math>, and their corresponding heaps (i.e., merging <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(A)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(A)</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(B)</annotation></semantics></math> and updating the average linkage weights of <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">all</span> edges affected by the merge).
We maintain the invariant that the heaps always store the correct average linkage weights of all edges.
We first compute <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\mathcal{I}(A,B)=N(A)\cap N(B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∩</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathcal{I}(A,B)=N(A)\cap N(B)</annotation></semantics></math>, which can be done by sorting both sets and merging the sorted arrays.
For each <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C\in\mathcal{I}(A,B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C\in\mathcal{I}(A,B)</annotation></semantics></math>, without loss of generality, we remove the reference to <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> in the heap and update the priority of <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math> to reflect the new average linkage weight from <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m9" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math> to <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m10" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A\cup B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A\cup B</annotation></semantics></math> using <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.1.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">decrease-key</span>.
Similarly, we update each <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m11" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C\in N(A)\setminus\mathcal{I}(A,B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∖</mo><mrow><mi class="ltx_font_mathcaligraphic">ℐ</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C\in N(A)\setminus\mathcal{I}(A,B)</annotation></semantics></math> to reference <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m12" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math> instead of <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m13" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math>.
Finally, for each <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m14" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C\in N(A)\cup N(B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∪</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C\in N(A)\cup N(B)</annotation></semantics></math> we update the priority of the edge <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m15" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(C,B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>C</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(C,B)</annotation></semantics></math> to reflect the new size of <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p1.m16" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.1" class="ltx_p">At the end of the merge: (1) <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(B)</annotation></semantics></math> contains <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(A)\cup N(B)\setminus\{A,B\}" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>∪</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>∖</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(A)\cup N(B)\setminus\{A,B\}</annotation></semantics></math>; (2) all edges in the updated <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(B)</annotation></semantics></math> have their average linkage weight correctly set; and (3) after the update all <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C\in N(B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>C</mi><mo>∈</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C\in N(B)</annotation></semantics></math> no longer reference <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math>, but point to <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math> and have their average linkage weights correctly set.
The cost of this merge procedure is dominated by the cost of sorting <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.m7" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(A)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(A)</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px2.p2.m8" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(B)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(B)</annotation></semantics></math>; plugging in the cost bounds for any reasonable heap data structure (e.g., a Fibonacci heap) yields the following lemma:</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmlemma6" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_lemma">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmlemma6.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Lemma 6</span></span><span id="Thmlemma6.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmlemma6.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmlemma6.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmlemma6.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">Merging two clusters <math id="Thmlemma6.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A,B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A,B</annotation></semantics></math> with <math id="Thmlemma6.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(A)+N(B)=T" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>A</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>B</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mi>T</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(A)+N(B)=T</annotation></semantics></math> can be done in <math id="Thmlemma6.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(T\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>T</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(T\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> time.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
</section>
<section id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Nearest-Neighbor Chain Analysis</h4>

<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p1.1" class="ltx_p">As we run the nearest-neighbor chain algorithm (Algorithm <a href="#alg4" title="Algorithm 4 ‣ Height of the dendrogram is 
          
            
              O
              
                
                
                  (
                  
                    
                      
                      log
                      2
                    
                    n
                  
                  ) ‣ 5 Average Linkage HAC on Paths in NC ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">4</span></a>), the algorithm only requires two non-trivial operations beyond basic data structures (e.g., stacks and heaps):<span id="footnote2" class="ltx_note ltx_role_footnote"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">2</sup><span class="ltx_note_outer"><span class="ltx_note_content"><sup class="ltx_note_mark">2</sup>
              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_note">2</span>
              
              
              
              
              
              
              
            In fact the same two primitives are also the only non-trivial ones used for the heap-based algorithm (Algorithm <a href="#alg5" title="Algorithm 5 ‣ Nearest-Neighbor Chain Analysis ‣ 6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
                  
                    log
                    n
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>) and thus we fully specify the details for this algorithm as well.</span></span></span></p>
<ol id="S6.I1" class="ltx_enumerate">
<li id="S6.I1.i1" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">1.</span> 
<div id="S6.I1.i1.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.I1.i1.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.I1.i1.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">BestEdge</span>(<math id="S6.I1.i1.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math>): fetch the highest similarity edge incident to a cluster <math id="S6.I1.i1.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div></li>
<li id="S6.I1.i2" class="ltx_item" style="list-style-type:none;"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_item">2.</span> 
<div id="S6.I1.i2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.I1.i2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.I1.i2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Merge</span>(<math id="S6.I1.i2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A,B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A,B</annotation></semantics></math>): merge the clusters <math id="S6.I1.i2.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A,B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A,B</annotation></semantics></math> into a cluster <math id="S6.I1.i2.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A\cup B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>A</mi><mo>∪</mo><mi>B</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A\cup B</annotation></semantics></math> (represented, without loss of generality by <math id="S6.I1.i2.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math>). Thus after the merge, <math id="S6.I1.i2.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="A" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>A</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A</annotation></semantics></math> is inactive, and <math id="S6.I1.i2.p1.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="B" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>B</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">B</annotation></semantics></math> remains active.</p>
</div></li>
</ol>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.1" class="ltx_p">Using the data structures above, we can implement both operations very quickly.
In particular, Lemma <a href="#Thmlemma6" title="Lemma 6. ‣ Merging clusters ‣ 6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
                  
                    log
                    n
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a> gives the time for merging two clusters, and <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">BestEdge</span>(<math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="C" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>C</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">C</annotation></semantics></math>) can be implemented in <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(1)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(1)</annotation></semantics></math> time using the <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p2.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">find-min</span> operation.</p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3.1" class="ltx_p">After merging two clusters that are reciprocal “best” neighbors, the algorithm back-tracks to the third vertex in the chain and restarts the process, using calls to <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">BestEdge</span>.
The cost of these calls can be charged to the merges, resulting in a total of <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>n</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(n)</annotation></semantics></math> time for the <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="n-1" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n-1</annotation></semantics></math> merges.
The time that still must be accounted for is that of <span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p3.1.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_smallcaps">Merge</span>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmclaim2" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_claim">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmclaim2.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Claim 2</span></span><span id="Thmclaim2.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmclaim2.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmclaim2.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmclaim2.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">The total amount time spent to perform all merges made by the nearest-neighbor chain algorithm is <math id="Thmclaim2.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(mh\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>h</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(mh\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.1" class="ltx_proof">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_font_italic ltx_title_proof">Proof.</h6>
<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.1.1" class="ltx_text">Split each edge <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(u,v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(u,v)</annotation></semantics></math> into two directed edges <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(u,v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(u,v)</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(v,u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(v,u)</annotation></semantics></math>, since the edge will be present initially in both <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(u)</annotation></semantics></math> and <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p4.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="N(v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N(v)</annotation></semantics></math> (and the intermediary clusters they form until they are merged).</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.1.1" class="ltx_text">Consider a directed edge <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="(u,v)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>v</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(u,v)</annotation></semantics></math>, and the merges it experiences as it flows along the path from the initial cluster containing it (<math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="u" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>u</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">u</annotation></semantics></math>) to the cluster where it is finally merged.
The dendrogram that the algorithm constructs has height <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="h" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>h</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h</annotation></semantics></math> by definition, and thus the length of this path is at most <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="h" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>h</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h</annotation></semantics></math>.
In each node on the path, the edge contributes a cost of <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> to the merge cost in Lemma <a href="#Thmlemma6" title="Lemma 6. ‣ Merging clusters ‣ 6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
                  
                    log
                    n
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">6</span></a>.
Thus, the cost of this edge across the entire path is at most <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p5.m6" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(h\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>h</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(h\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.</span></p>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p6" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p6.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p6.1.1" class="ltx_text">Applying this charging argument for all <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p6.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="2m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>m</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2m</annotation></semantics></math> directed edges, the total cost over all merges is <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p6.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(mh\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>h</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(mh\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.
∎</span></p>
</div>
</div>
<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p7" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px3.p7.1" class="ltx_p">Putting together the above yields the following theorem.
See <a href="#Thmtheorem5" title="Theorem 5. ‣ 1.1 Our Contributions ‣ 1 Introduction ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a></p>
</div>

              <span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_listingline">5</span>

</figure>
</section>
<section id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px4" class="ltx_paragraph">
<h4 class="ltx_title ltx_title_paragraph">Heap-Based Algorithm</h4>

<div id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.1" class="ltx_p">We note it is easy to extend the argument above to obtain a similar bound for the heap-based algorithm
(pseudocode shown in Algorithm <a href="#alg5" title="Algorithm 5 ‣ Nearest-Neighbor Chain Analysis ‣ 6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
                  
                    log
                    n
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>).
In particular, beyond the cost for merging, which is the same in the nearest-neighbor chain algorithm and the heap-based algorithm (captured by Claim <a href="#Thmclaim2" title="Claim 2. ‣ Nearest-Neighbor Chain Analysis ‣ 6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
                  
                    log
                    n
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">2</span></a>), the only extra work incurred by the heap-based algorithm is due to edges unsuccessfully extracted from the heap (Line <a href="#alg5" title="Algorithm 5 ‣ Nearest-Neighbor Chain Analysis ‣ 6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
                  
                    log
                    n
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a> of Algorithm <a href="#alg5" title="Algorithm 5 ‣ Nearest-Neighbor Chain Analysis ‣ 6 
        
          
            O
            
              
              
                (
                
                  ⁢
                  m
                  h
                  
                    log
                    n
                  
                
                )
              
            
          
        
       Upper Bound ‣ It’s Hard to HAC with Average Linkage!" class="ltx_ref"><span class="ltx_text ltx_ref_tag">5</span></a>).
The number of such unsuccessful edges is at most <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="m" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>m</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">m</annotation></semantics></math>, and each such edge incurs <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> work due to an extra call to <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.m3" class="ltx_Math" alttext="\textsc{BestEdge}(u)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mtext class="ltx_font_smallcaps">BestEdge</mtext><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>u</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\textsc{BestEdge}(u)</annotation></semantics></math>, for a total of <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.m4" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(m\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(m\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> extra work.
Thus the total work of the heap-based algorithm is also <math id="S6.SS0.SSS0.Px4.p1.m5" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(mh\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>h</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(mh\log n)</annotation></semantics></math>.</p>
</div>
<div id="Thmtheorem10" class="ltx_theorem ltx_theorem_theorem">
<h6 class="ltx_title ltx_runin ltx_title_theorem"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_theorem"><span id="Thmtheorem10.2" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">Theorem 10</span></span><span id="Thmtheorem10.3" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">.</span></h6>
<div id="Thmtheorem10.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="Thmtheorem10.p1.1" class="ltx_p"><span id="Thmtheorem10.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">There is an implementation of the heap-based algorithm for average linkage HAC that runs in <math id="Thmtheorem10.p1.m1" class="ltx_Math" alttext="O(mh\log n)" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mrow><mi>O</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mrow><mi>m</mi><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>h</mi><mo lspace="0.167em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mi>log</mi><mo lspace="0.167em">⁡</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">O(mh\log n)</annotation></semantics></math> time where <math id="Thmtheorem10.p1.m2" class="ltx_Math" alttext="h" display="inline" intent=":literal"><semantics><mi>h</mi><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">h</annotation></semantics></math> is the height of the output dendrogram.</span></p>
</div>
</div>
</section>
</section>
<section id="S7" class="ltx_section">
<h2 class="ltx_title ltx_title_section"><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_section">7 </span>Conclusion</h2>

<div id="S7.p1" class="ltx_para">
<p id="S7.p1.1" class="ltx_p">In this paper, we studied the parallel and sequential complexity of hierarchical
graph clustering. We gave new classic and fine-grained reductions for Hierarchical Agglomerative Clustering (HAC) under the average linkage
measure that likely rule out efficient algorithms for <span id="S7.p1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_italic">exact</span> average linkage, parallel or otherwise.
We also showed that such impossibility results can be circumvented if the output dendrogram has low height or is a path. An interesting question is whether such structure can be leveraged for other variants of interest of average linkage HAC: for example, can we can obtain dynamic algorithms for HAC that are also parameterized by the height?</p>
</div>
<div class="ltx_pagination ltx_role_newpage"></div>
</section>

    <script>
    // Auxiliary function, building the preview feature when
    // an inline citation is clicked
    function clicked_cite(e) {
      e.preventDefault();
      let cite = this.closest('.ltx_cite');
      let next = cite.nextSibling;
      if (next && next.nodeType == Node.ELEMENT_NODE && next.getAttribute('class') == "ar5iv-bibitem-preview") {
        next.remove();
        return; }
      // Before adding a preview modal,
      // cleanup older previews, in case they're still open
      document.querySelectorAll('span.ar5iv-bibitem-preview').forEach(function(node) {
        node.remove();
      })

      // Create the preview
      preview = document.createElement('span');
      preview.setAttribute('class','ar5iv-bibitem-preview');
      let target = document.getElementById(this.getAttribute('href').slice(1));
      target.childNodes.forEach(function (child) {
        preview.append(child.cloneNode(true));
      });
      let close_x = document.createElement('button');
      close_x.setAttribute("aria-label","Close modal for bibliography item preview");
      close_x.textContent = "×";
      close_x.setAttribute('class', 'ar5iv-button-close-preview');
      close_x.setAttribute('onclick','this.parentNode.remove()');
      preview.append(close_x);
      preview.querySelectorAll('.ltx_tag_bibitem').forEach(function(node) {
        node.remove();
      });
      cite.parentNode.insertBefore(preview, cite.nextSibling);
      return;
    }
    // Global Document initialization:
    // - assign the preview feature to all inline citation links
    document.querySelectorAll(".ltx_cite .ltx_ref").forEach(function (link) {
      link.addEventListener("click", clicked_cite);
    });
    </script>
    </body>
</html>
